- **AG4 Item 8 sort:** Name (default), Source size, Status. Status order: errors, warnings, undecided, ok. Ties break by name. Saved in gui.toml.
- **AG5 Item 2 Ctrl+Z in Meshes:** enable it unless `app.rs:169` documents a reason. If it does, keep it off and quote the reason in the report.
- **AG6 Item 6 taskbar progress:** skip it unless `windows` crate `ITaskbarList3` is already in the lock with the right features. Say which.
- **AG7 Item 13 streaming thumbnails:** only if the plan worker can send per-file results without reordering the grid. Otherwise do the progress bar only and record why.
- **AG8 Item 10 pin:** one pin, held in memory. Dropped on Close, on a texture change and on folder open.
- **AG9 Colour field conventions (Task 1):** one widget, `ui::color::color_field` (egui swatch popup + `#RRGGBB` box). TruePBR colours (`emissive_color`, `subsurface_color`, `coat_color`, `fuzz.color`) keep integer sRGB bytes 0-255 and the number boxes; swatch and hex commit through the same `set_json_value` edit (same merge, undo step, reset). The 3D View background is one sRGB grey (`Look::grey`, perceptual 0..1): swatch and hex edit it, and a non-grey pick or hex is stored as its Rec.709 luma. Sun, point light and radius are brightness values, not colours, so they get no picker. A hex box reverts to the stored value on focus loss when invalid. **Amended by AG133:** the TruePBR colours are float channels now, with float boxes in place of the 0-255 ones.

- **AG10 Esc in the colour hex box (Task 2, Task 1 review):** Esc cancels the edit: the box reverts and nothing is committed (it matches item 4, Esc is Cancel). The edit buffer carries the frame it was last drawn in; one older than the previous frame belongs to a row that went away and is dropped.
- **AG11 Dialog keys (Task 2, item 4).** The shared `dialogs::modal_keys` returns `cancel` (Esc, or a click outside where allowed) and `enter` (Enter while no widget has focus and not on the frame the dialog opened). Esc is the dialog's Cancel everywhere.
  - Click outside does nothing in: the rename dialogs (`set-rename`, and `rename_dialog` as before), Copy settings, Fill from names (its ticks, AG99) and the undecided-textures list (Proceed anyway; it has open combo popups and per-row choices a stray click would drop). It cancels in every other dialog.
  - Enter: Save in Close/Open unsaved; Close in Export blocked; OK in Error; Keep running in Export running; Keep it in Overwrite JSON; Cancel in the Reset all and Delete set confirms. External change: Enter is Cancel, which keeps the edits in memory and leaves the file on disk alone (not Reload, which would drop unsaved edits; never Overwrite). Proceed anyway, Export warnings (N3) and Copy settings: Enter does nothing. Rename keeps its own Enter = Apply. Fill from names: Enter is Fill (AG99).
- **AG12 Undo visibility (Task 2, item 2).** Header Undo/Redo buttons, disabled by `can_undo`/`can_redo`, hover "Undo: <label>  (Ctrl+Z)". The status line shows "Undid: <label>." or "Redid: <label>." in front, and it survives the re-plan that follows; the next edit or a new history drops it. It stands beside the Export-cancelled note (AF10, AF11), which stays; the encoding note (Z11) is still cleared by an undo. **AG5 outcome:** Ctrl+Z/Ctrl+Y now work in the Meshes view. `app.rs` carried no documented reason for the `meshes_view_active` guard (its only comment was "leave a text field's own undo alone and wait while a dialog is open", which still holds); the key handling moved to `ui::header::undo_redo_keys`.
- **AG13 Delete set and Rename (Task 2, items 4 and 23).** Delete set asks "Delete set '<id>' (<N> members)? Its textures stay; only the set is removed." with Delete / Cancel; the deletion is still one undo step. The set menu groups Rename…, Rename outputs…  F2 and Rename inputs… under a "Rename" submenu, each with a one-line hover text, built from one data array (`rename_entries` in `ui/sets_column.rs`). The multi-selection menus (`rename_entries` in `model/bulk.rs`) are unchanged.
- **AG14 Hover text (Task 2, item 24):** added to the Mode combo and its entries, the four Textures filter toggles, the preview channel buttons, Split, Fit, 1:1 and Live encode. Live encode's wording follows docs/gui.md; the others are new one-liners.
- **AG15 Undo note lifetime (Task 3 carry-over, refines AG12).** The "Undid/Redid" note stands in front of the normal status text ("Undid: x. 7 textures, 3 sets"), not in place of it. It stays through the plan the undo started and goes with the next plan that lands, or 5 s after it was set (`tick`), whichever comes first. An edit or a new history still drops it at once.
- **AG16 Keys behind a sets-column box (Task 3 carry-over).** Ctrl+Z/Ctrl+Y wait while a sets-column box (Delete set, drop choice, Replace, Refused, Rename) is open, as they do for `model.dialog`. Enter presses OK on the Refused box, as it does on the Error dialog.
- **AG17 Window and layout memory (Task 3, item 3, AG3).** Everything goes in `gui.toml` under `[layout]` (`settings/layout.rs`), at the existing `%APPDATA%\sdc\gui.toml`; eframe's `persistence` stays off. Saved: window inner size and outer position (points, with the scale they were taken at) and the maximised flag; the widths of the Textures, Sets, set 3D, Meshes tree and Advanced panels; the Textures | Meshes view; the Textures filter toggles (`needs-decision`, `warnings`, `failed`, `not-in-set`); the preview channel (`Channel::shader_id`) and layout (`side-by-side`, `split`); the Meshes view's and the set preview's 3D Look. Not saved: zoom, selection, the open folder, the preview's mip/heatmap/split position. Maximised keeps the last normal rectangle; minimised and fullscreen change nothing. A missing or old file gives 1400x900 and today's widths; the 2D | 3D switch (AB4) is unchanged and defaults to 2D. Each field is read on its own (`lenient`): a wrong type, a width under 200, an unknown filter or panel name, channel 99 or an unknown layout is dropped alone and never fails the file; look values are clamped to their ranges. The panel widths are read each frame from egui's memory (`chosen` for the two columns, `PanelState` for the rest) and put back before the first frame (`SdcApp::restore_ctx`); a change under 0.5 pt is noise.
- **AG18 Monitor approach (Task 3).** The check runs before the window exists, so winit/eframe monitor info is not available yet; it uses the Windows API through `windows-sys` 0.61.2 (already in Cargo.lock for winit; added to the gui crate for Windows only, features Foundation, Gdi, HiDpi): `EnumDisplayMonitors` + `GetMonitorInfoW`, work areas in pixels, primary first. The process is made per-monitor DPI aware first (winit does the same a moment later) so the rectangles are real pixels. `layout::fit_window` is pure: a saved rectangle (points times the saved scale = pixels) that shows less than 64x64 px on every monitor is centred on the primary monitor at its saved size, capped to 90% of it; one that shows enough stays, except a side bigger than all monitors together shrinks to them. No monitor info (non-Windows, API failure): the size is applied and the OS places the window. The position is not applied after the first frame; a monitor with a different scale than the saved one maps the point size through the saved scale, which can be a little off until the user moves the window once.
- **AG19 Debounce (Task 3).** `SaveDebounce`: the first change starts a 2 s wait, later changes inside it ride along, one write when it ends (`SdcApp::sync_layout_at`, called each frame with `Instant::now()`; a repaint is requested for the end of the wait). `on_exit` writes a still-pending change. A write failure is ignored as the other settings saves are. Other `save_settings` calls write the whole file, current layout included.
- **AG20 Test seam (Task 3).** Tests drive `sync_layout_at(ctx, now)` with their own clock and a temp `gui.toml`; none touches the real `%APPDATA%`.
- **AG21 Layout writes and the title bar (Task 3 review carry-over, refines AG18, AG19).** The first frame's capture is the starting point: what it reads that `gui.toml` did not have (a window, a panel width) is taken into the settings without starting the 2 s wait, so a start where nothing changed writes nothing. Each frame compares a small `layout::Probe` (viewport rectangles and flags, the panel widths, the view, the filters, the channel, the layout, both looks) with the last one and captures only when it changed, so an idle frame allocates nothing. `fit_window` also keeps the top 32 px of the window on a monitor (with at least 64 px of width): a title bar off every monitor moves down or up onto the monitor that shows the most of the window. The 2D preview's settings panel (`preview-settings`) joins the saved widths; it is fixed at 280 pt today, so its saved width changes nothing yet. The undo note's 5 s (AG15) asks for a frame when it runs out (`undo_note_left`), so an idle window drops it on time.
- **AG22 One action list (Task 4, items 5 and 19).** `ui::actions::ACTIONS` is a static table: `id`, `label`, `area` (Global, Textures grid, Preview, Sets, Dialogs), `keys`, `palette` (offered in the palette), `enabled`, `run: Option<fn(&mut AppModel, &egui::Context)>`. `keys` is a slice, not one optional shortcut: Redo keeps Ctrl+Y and Ctrl+Shift+Z, the sheet has F1 and ?, the preview opens with Enter and Space. `run: None` marks a key handled where it belongs and only listed (a dialog's Enter and Esc, `modal_keys`). It lives in `ui` because `run` needs the egui context (the Textures | Meshes view is in egui memory) and Open folder needs the file picker. The F1 sheet, the Ctrl+K palette and the key handling read the table; the menus keep their own lists but take a shortcut's text from it (`keys_text`: the Undo/Redo hover, the preview's Close hover, "Rename outputs…  F2" in the set and multi-selection menus). docs/gui.md's Keyboard table is `markdown_table()`'s output and a test keeps them equal. A key may appear twice only in areas that are never live together (`Area::exclusive_with`: Esc clears the selection in the grid, closes the preview, cancels a dialog; Enter opens the preview from the grid and presses a dialog's default). Grid and Sets are live together.
- **AG23 When keys act (Task 4).** One dispatcher, `actions::keys`, runs first in the frame (top of `ui::draw_with_scan`; `app.rs` calls it in the Meshes view), before any widget has seen the input, and matches the keys with the most modifiers first (egui ignores an extra Shift). First, because egui's text field deletes to the end of the line on Ctrl+K, and a key that runs an action must not also reach a widget. Ctrl+S, Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Y moved into it from `app.rs` (`header::undo_redo_keys` is gone). Nothing fires while `model.dialog`, a sets-column box, any popup or menu, or any egui modal (the palette, the sheet, Rename, the slot commands) is open; Ctrl+S and Undo/Redo now wait for popups and modals and for a focused field too, where Ctrl+S used to fire anywhere. While a widget has the keyboard focus (Task 2's guard, `egui_wants_keyboard_input`) only Ctrl+K fires. egui drops the focus on Esc before any widget runs, so a field focused at the previous frame's start still owns this frame's Esc: Esc only leaves the field (before, Esc in the search box also cleared the selection). Grid and Sets keys act in the Textures view with no texture expanded, Preview keys while one is.
- **AG24 Grid keys (Task 4, item 5).** The arrows move in the displayed order (search and filters applied). Left and Right step one texture; Up and Down step the number of tiles in a row as the grid was last drawn (`Nav::cols`). A step off the grid does nothing; with no texture to start from, any arrow selects the first. They start from the texture the keys last moved to while it is still selected, else the active texture. Shift+arrow is a Shift-click on the texture moved to (F3a D1): the range runs from the active texture, which stays the anchor, and the moving end is kept in `Nav::cursor`. Ctrl+arrow is not added: F3a has no "focus without select". Enter or Space opens the active texture's preview. A texture the keys or the palette move to scrolls into view: `show_rows` draws only the rows on screen, so its rectangle is worked out from the first drawn row and handed to `scroll_to_rect`.
- **AG25 Preview keys (Task 4, item 5).** `[` and `]` expand the previous or next texture in the displayed order, no wrap; one hidden by a filter is skipped. 1-6 pick the channel buttons left to right (RGBA, R, G, B, A, α-mask, which are shader ids 0-5). PageDown steps to the next, smaller mip and PageUp back, within the previewed mips; the zoom follows as the slider's does. The mips come from `AppModel::preview_mips`, which the toolbar now uses too. Esc is unchanged (now a table entry).
- **AG26 Palette and sheet (Task 4, items 5 and 19).** F1, or ? with no field focused, opens the sheet: a `modal_keys` dialog listing every action with a key under Global, Textures grid, Preview, Sets, Dialogs; Esc, Enter or Close shuts it. Ctrl+K opens the palette, from a text field too: a search field (it keeps the focus while the palette is open), then the enabled actions whose area applies (the arrow moves and channel keys are left out; disabled ones are hidden, not greyed), the textures by file name and the sets by label. Matching is case-insensitive: prefix, then substring, then subsequence; results sort by that rank, then actions before textures before sets, then name, and stop at 200 (`show_rows`). The texture and set search is cached by query and the plan's and documents' revisions (`view_key`), so a frame with an unchanged query searches nothing. Up and Down move, Enter or a click runs the action or picks the texture or set as a click does, scrolled into view in its column; a pick made in the Meshes view switches to the Textures view first. Esc closes. Both draw with the dialogs, so both views have them.
- **AG27 NiAlphaProperty keys and the schema source (Task 4b).** The vendored `crates/gui/assets/pbr.schema.json` is now the upstream file byte for byte, fetched 2026-10-14 from https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hakasapl/PGPatcher/refs/heads/main/schema/pbr.schema.json (the owner supplied the four new keys and then the URL). The only difference from the old copy is `alphaprop_flags`, `alphaprop_thresh`, `alphaprop_create` and `alphaprop_delete`, which sit after `zbuffer_write` and before `lock_diffuse`; `KEYS` follows that order. The flags and threshold are `Number(NumRange::Int(65535 | 255))`: a whole number, clamped to its range and always written as a JSON integer; create and delete are `OnlyTrue` (written only when true, default false). The pgpsim range test skips `Int` keys because pgpsim (NIF-owned) does not simulate `alphaprop_*` yet; `tests/alpha_prop.rs` covers them.
- **AG28 Defaults.** Switching "Override flags" on writes 4844 (testing on, Greater, Src Alpha / Inv Src Alpha, blending off); switching the threshold on writes 128; both are PGPatcher's own create defaults. Off removes the key. The flag fields follow the Gamebryo / NifSkope layout (bit 0 blending, 1-4 source, 5-8 destination, 9 testing, 10-12 function, 13 no sorter); bits 14-15 are kept as stored. `nif::props::Alpha` reads bits 0 and 9 the same way.
- **AG29 The card section.** `NiAlphaProperty` is a collapsible group on the TruePBR card (open when any alpha key is set), between the visual groups and Matching. Each change of a flag control is one `set_json_value` of the composed integer, so merge key, undo, provenance and reset work as for the other keys. The "edit raw" box takes 0-65535 on Enter or focus loss; other text is outlined red and not written, Esc cancels. Delete greys the flags, threshold and create controls (hover: "Delete takes priority over the other alpha keys of this entry"); a stored value stays as it was.
- **AG30 Pitfalls.** `alphaprop_flags` or `alphaprop_thresh` set while `alphaprop_create` is off warns that it only changes shapes that already have an NiAlphaProperty. `alphaprop_delete` on together with any other alpha key warns that Delete wins. Like the other pitfalls they are reported at export, never corrected.
- **AG31 JSON editor.** The JSON editor needs no change: it lists the keys from `KEYS`, and the shared `control` draws the integer boxes; a key added from its + menu starts at 4844 / 128 / true.
- **AG131 No sorter has no effect in Skyrim SE (RenderDoc capture, 2026-10-15; placed beside AG27-AG31, numbered in order).** Capture frame15011 (`tools/capture-plan-nosorter.md`, "Result"): two blended hairs drawn in the forward blended pass, event 5029 (HairMaleImperial1, bit 13 off, flags 0x12ED) and event 5060 (HairFemaleDarkElf01, bit 13 on, 0x32ED), 20,754 indices each in separate index buffers. The two index lists are byte-identical (sha256 860fde25…) and each equals the NIF's stored triangle list verbatim (offset 346447 in both files). So the game does not sort the triangles of an alpha-blended shape, and NiAlphaProperty bit 13 ("No Sorter") changes nothing. Limit: GPU-side sorting is not excluded (Gamebryo's sorter is CPU-side). GUI: the card's checkbox reads "No sorter (no effect in Skyrim SE)", with the evidence in its hover, and is drawn last, after the raw box. The bit still round-trips (AG28), and nothing sets it automatically (the 4844 default has it off). The Meshes shape card's alpha line (AI5) never named the bit and is unchanged.
- **AG32 Keys honour "enabled" (Task 5 carry-over).** `actions::keys` runs an action only when its `enabled` says so, as the palette does: Ctrl+S on a clean project, Ctrl+Z with no history and Enter with no single texture selected do nothing and are not consumed. Up and Down (and Shift+Up/Down) are enabled only when the Textures grid drew last frame: `nav.cols` is 0 until the grid draws and `draw_with_scan` zeroes it every frame after the keys, so a hidden or collapsed Textures column, or one that has not drawn yet, leaves Up and Down off instead of stepping by a default width. Ctrl+S swallowed by a focused text field (AG23) sets the status line to "Ctrl+S: leave the text field first" when there is something to save.
- **AG33 Alpha card follow-ups (Task 4b review).** Every alpha key that is set has a reset button (the same one `row()` gives the other keys). `alphaprop_*` numbers are read with `as_f64` (`NumRange::whole`): `4844.0` is 4844; a negative, fractional or too large stored value is "set and invalid" (its box stays ticked, an amber note names the range) and not "not set"; the JSON editor shares this through `control`. The raw-flags edit buffer carries a frame stamp like the colour hex box. `NumRange::Int` notes the range ("clamped to a whole number 0-65535"). The "Delete takes priority" hover is on the Delete checkbox itself.
- **AG34 Explorer requests (Task 5, items 6 and 14).** Open export folder runs `explorer.exe <folder>`; Show in Explorer runs `explorer.exe /select,<file>`. The model only queues a `ShellRequest` (`model::shell`); `App::update` spawns it detached (`DETACHED_PROCESS`, never waited on, since Explorer exits 1 on success) so tests see the queue and never start Explorer. `/select,` and the path are ONE argument built by the pure `select_arg` (separators normalised to `\`), and `Command::arg` quotes it as a whole, so spaces, commas and non-ASCII names stay inside it. No shell is involved. Open export folder is off, with the reason on hover, while the export folder does not exist; Show in Explorer is off, with the reason, when its file is not on disk. Entries: the texture tile menu (one texture selected), the slot menu (so the slot card and the pill member menus), a submenu of members in the set pill menu (drawn before the editing-disable, so it works read-only), and each Files row (the written output when it exists, otherwise the source). The slot menu keeps Lock / Unlock last; the entry sits before it.
- **AG35 Progress line (Task 5).** `done/total · elapsed · files/s · ETA` from the existing events only (no new engine events). `Pace` takes every time as an argument (a fake clock in tests). Done = rows no longer queued or running. Elapsed runs from `start_export` and stops at the end of the batch. Files/s is the number of finishes in the last 10 s divided by min(elapsed, 10 s). The ETA (remaining / files/s) is left out until 3 files have finished or 2 s have passed, and while nothing finished in the window. A finish is a FileDone, a FileFailed, or a skip that sets a row state. The drawer asks for a repaint every 500 ms while the export runs.
- **AG36 Taskbar progress (AG6) skipped.** `windows` 0.62 is in the lock only through eframe, wgpu and accesskit; `Win32_UI_Shell` and `Win32_System_Com` happen to be enabled there, but `ITaskbarList3` needs a direct dependency on `windows`, COM initialisation, a window handle and unsafe code, none of which can be checked headless. Left out until someone can try it on a screen.
- **AG37 Files list (item 14).** Virtualised with `ScrollArea::show_rows` over a cached index (`FilesIndex`) keyed by (filter, `ExportModel::revision`); with no filter there is no index and the list reads the rows directly. The revision is bumped by every event except Progress and BatchStarted, from a process-wide counter so a fresh export never reuses one. "Failed only" means `Failed` or validation failed; "Has warnings" means at least one warning of any severity. Both on is AND. The toolbar counts still scan all rows once per frame (about 10 000 comparisons); only the list is cached.
- **AG38 Warnings tab (item 14).** One row per warning, sorted by code then file: code, severity, the message (wrapped), the file as a link. Virtualised with `show_rows`, so rows have a fixed height of three lines; a longer message is clipped there and shown whole on hover (`warning_line`: `CODE (severity): message`). The list is cached by the revision. The grouped-by-code view is gone from the drawer (`ExportModel::by_code` stays for the reports).
- **AG39 Task 5 carry-over.** (a) A skip (up to date, needs decision, ...) counts in done/total but not in the files/s rate or the ETA: it supersedes the last sentence of AG35. (b) The Warnings tab has a "By code" toggle (flat list stays the default): one row per code with its count, expanding it lists that code's files; both views are virtualised, the by-code one as one flat virtual list of headers and expanded files. This supersedes the "grouped view is gone" line of AG38. In the flat list the file link comes before the message so it stays clickable in the clipped row. (c) The drawer's disk checks (export folder exists, row output/source is a file) go through `StatCache`, re-checked at most once a second, so a folder created while the drawer is open enables "Open export folder" within a second.
- **AG40 File pickers (Task 6, item 7).** Every picker goes through `model::picker`: `Picker::request` runs the dialog on a spawned thread and the result lands in an inbox tagged with a `PickerPurpose` (OpenFolder, ExportFolder, ChooseMo2, ScanFolder, NvttPath, SaveReport, ShaderFolder). No executor: `pollster` and `futures` are not direct dependencies (pollster is in the lock only through other crates), so the thread calls the plain blocking `rfd::FileDialog`, which is equivalent. There is no HDRI picker in the code, so there is no Hdri purpose. The inbox is not the `WorkerMsg` channel: the app model, the meshes model and each 3D viewport own separate channels and are built standalone in tests, so each drains its own purposes from the shared inbox in its `poll` (`AppModel` takes OpenFolder, ExportFolder, NvttPath, SaveReport; `MeshesModel` ChooseMo2 and ScanFolder; `Viewport3d` ShaderFolder).
- **AG41 One dialog at a time.** The picker state is shared (a thread-local `Picker::shared()`, so the UI thread has one and each test its own). While a dialog is open every picker button is disabled with the hover text "A file dialog is already open", `request` refuses a second one, and the `open` action reports `enabled == false`. The app keeps repainting while one is open and the thread asks for a repaint when the answer lands. A panicking dialog still clears the busy state.
- **AG42 Stale and cancelled results.** A cancel does nothing and clears the busy state. ExportFolder and SaveReport are tagged with the folder open when the dialog started and are dropped if another folder is open when the answer arrives (the export folder triggers a replan; the report is of that folder's export). OpenFolder was tagged too until AG129; it now always opens. ChooseMo2, ScanFolder, NvttPath and ShaderFolder are app-wide and never stale: they set a scan source or a tool, whatever folder is open.
- **AG43 Parent window not passed.** rfd can take a parent handle, but `RawWindowHandle` is not `Send`, so it cannot go to the dialog thread without unsafe wrapping. Skipped: the dialog is a top-level window and the main window is not modal while it is open.
- **AG44 Save report.** The report is written when the dialog answers (`AppModel::picked`), not in the drawer; a write error raises the same error dialog. Tests use a fake backend (`PickerBackend`), never a real dialog.
- **AG45 Fix round 1 (Task 6).** Results are routed by requester, not by purpose: each `Picker` handle has its own owner id (every model and every 3D viewport gets one from `Picker::shared()`), the dialog result is tagged with it, and `take()` returns only that owner's results. So a shader-folder pick made in the set preview reaches the set preview, not the Meshes viewport, and a purpose nobody claims cannot be taken by the wrong model (it stays in the inbox only if its requester is gone). A stale folder-scoped result now sets the status line to "Picker result ignored: the folder changed while the dialog was open".
- **AG46 Textures filters (Task 7, item 8).** The four toggles stay. Beside them are combo boxes: Sub-type, Output format, Encoder and Set membership (Any, In a set, Not in a set). Sub-type, format and encoder list Any and only the values present in the folder (the encoder list keeps the order bc7e, rgbcx, ispc (BC6H), NVTT, uncompressed; ispc is the BC6H backend, so it is there when a BC6H texture is). A saved value the folder lacks is still listed, so a restored filter is never invisible. A texture with no job (failed, skipped DDS) matches no combo value. Everything combines with AND, search included. "In a set" is the existing rule: a set of more than one member.
- **AG47 Sort.** Name (default), Source size, Status. Name is the order the grid always had (the project-relative key, folder then file); it is the tiebreak of the other two, because the sort is stable. Source size lists the largest first; the size is the job's source stamp, or a skipped DDS's own stamp, else 0 (a packed output has no single source). Status ranks errors (Failed, Error) first, then textures with a warning that counts toward the badge, then undecided, then the rest; the rank is checked in that order, so an undecided texture with a warning is in the warnings group.
- **AG48 Displayed order.** Arrow keys, Shift-range, `[` and `]` and Ctrl+A all read the one sorted, filtered list (`AppModel::visible_shared`). The selection is keyed, so it survives a sort or filter change, and F3a's "N selected (M hidden by filter)" count reads the same list.
- **AG49 Tile size.** A slider, 64 to 192 points, default 96, in `AppModel::prefs.tile`. The column count Up and Down use is worked out from it each frame. A tile never draws wider than the column. The narrowest column stays sized for the 96 default.
- **AG50 Saved state.** `[layout]` in gui.toml gets `filter_subtype`, `filter_format`, `filter_encoder`, `filter_set` (`any`, `in`, `not-in`), `sort` (`name`, `size`, `status`) and `tile`. They go through Task 3's probe, capture and 2 s debounce. A bad value falls back to its default on its own (a tile size out of range gives 96).
- **AG51 Empty state.** When the filters or the search hide every texture, the column says "No textures match the filters (N hidden)" with a **Clear filters** button. Clear filters resets the four toggles, the four combos and the search; not the sort or the tile size. With no textures at all it still says "No textures to show."
- **AG52 Cache.** The index list and the combo options are cached: the list by (filters, search, sort, items revision), the options by the items revision. It is built once per change, not per frame (10,000 textures). A tile size change does not rebuild it. `visible_items()` stays as a plain copy for tests.
- **AG53 Compact combos.** Each combo shows its name and choice in one box ("Encoder: bc7e") and carries hover text. A combo is a nested layout that `horizontal_wrapped` cannot wrap, so the rows are cut by the column's width, which keeps the narrowest column narrow (column_widths tests).
- **AG54 Texel readout (Task 8, item 1).** A strip under the viewports (in the notes panel), not a panel that follows the cursor. It reads the CPU images the preview holds: the source level of the result on show (the loaded source before any encode) and the result's decoded level, at the mip on show; never the GPU. A side it lacks shows "—". The texel is the one under the cursor at the shown mip; side by side, the cursor's fraction of its pane is used for both panes, and in Split both sides share the one rect, so both always read one texel. The strip is laid out before the viewports, so it shows what the last frame measured and asks for a repaint when that changed. It keeps a fixed five-line height with each line cut rather than wrapped, so the viewports never move as the pointer enters them.
- **AG55 Readout numbers and colour space.** The numbers are the stored values (what the preview decodes; the GPU textures are UNORM too). 0–255 is the stored float rounded; the float is shown with 4 decimals. The sRGB / UNORM toggle only changes how the view reads the bytes, so the readout does not convert: its first line names the space ("stored values, sRGB-encoded" or "stored values, read as linear (UNORM)"). HDR formats show floats only ("linear HDR values", no 0–255). The error is |source − encoded| per channel; "max" is the error the channel measures, the heatmap's rule (RGBA: the largest of R, G, B). A channel button narrows the lines to that channel (α-mask: A).
- **AG56 Normal readout.** For sub-types of category Normal: both vectors unpacked from 0..1 to −1..1, the encoded one's z rebuilt from x and y for BC5 and RG8 as the shader does; the encoded length; and the angle between the source and encoded vectors in degrees ("—" when either has zero length). It ignores the channel and view toggles.
- **AG57 Lens.** "Lens" toggle and the `L` key (Preview area). A 160-point circle at 8× the view's zoom, centred on the centre of the texel under the cursor (the crosshair marks it). Off the picture the texel clamps to the edge; tiled, it wraps. The preview is a wgpu paint callback, not an egui texture, so the lens is a second draw of the pane's slot (source, encoded or heatmap) with its own uniform block and a nearest-sampling bind group created with the slot; the shader keeps only the inscribed circle. No texture is uploaded for it. It is drawn in both panes; in Split each side of the divider shows its own side.
- **AG58 Heatmap job.** The heatmap is made by `spawn_guarded` like the other preview work, keyed by (result `Arc`, mip, channel, full scale); the result `Arc` stands for the texture and its encode revision. One job runs at a time: a key change while one runs waits for it, its result is dropped by key, and the next frame starts the current key. Jobs carry an id that keeps counting across texture changes, so a late result of a dropped job never lands. The heatmap on show stays until the current key's lands (a new mip's old heatmap is drawn stretched over the picture meanwhile). A texture change drops it. The job is not part of `busy()`, which locks edits (the settings, bulk and Advanced panels); `heatmap_pending()` keeps frames coming until it lands. A panicked job shows the error dialog and leaves that key without a heatmap rather than retrying it every frame.
- **AG59 Heatmap legend and full scale.** A colour bar from 0 to the full scale with ticks at 0, half and full, and a logarithmic slider 0.005 to 0.5, default 0.1 (the old hard-coded value). Shown in the notes strip while the heatmap is on show. Neither the full scale nor the lens, tiling or exposure is saved in gui.toml (the layout probe copies named fields only).
- **AG60 Exposure.** A slider −8 to +8 EV, step 0.1, shown when the tone map is on: when the output format is HDR (BC6H, RGBA16F). The source pane is tone-mapped under the same condition, so an HDR source encoded to an HDR format gets it too; an HDR source encoded to an SDR format is not tone-mapped today and gets no slider. A uniform carries 2^EV, multiplied in before Reinhard; EV 0 draws exactly as before. Reset to 0 on every texture change (`preview_open`).
- **AG61 Tiling.** "Tile 3×3" toggle and the `T` key (free in the action table). The shader takes uv −1..2 and wraps it, with the same rule as `view::tile_uv`; zoom, pan, the readout and the lens all work in that uv. *Fit* fits the nine copies while tiling is on; toggling does not refit.
- **AG62 Heatmap per mip (Task 8 carry-over; refines AG58).** The heatmap on show stays while a new one is made only for the **same mip**. After a mip change the plain encoded image shows until the heatmap for the new mip lands, never the old mip stretched over it, and the legend label reads "Heatmap |Δ| updating…" greyed.
- **AG63 Slider drag never blanks the heatmap (Task 8 carry-over).** A job that lands for an older key shows if only its full scale differs (same encode, mip and channel); the next frame starts the job for the current key. One job runs at a time, and a new one starts only when the wanted key differs from the one on show and none is in flight. The label says "updating…" until the current key lands.
- **AG64 Pin (item 10, plan AG8).** "Pin this result" in the expanded 2D toolbar and the key `P` (`Area::Preview`, action `pin`, on only while `can_pin`). The pin holds the `Arc` of the finished encode, so its decoded images and the GPU upload are shared with nothing re-encoded and nothing copied. The GPU texture for the pin (`Slot::Pinned`) exists only while the pin is compared.
- **AG65 Only a finished encode can be pinned.** `can_pin` needs `EncodedState::Done` and a job on the texture. A stale or encoding result does not match the settings now on show, so its summary would be false.
- **AG66 One pin; when it goes.** Pinning again replaces the pin (the old images are released). `PreviewModel::clear` drops it, so closing the preview, a texture change and a folder open all drop it; a settings change, a re-plan with the same texture, and a size-limit change keep it. Pin ids never repeat within a session (`next_pin` survives `clear`). *Unpin* drops it too.
- **AG67 Compare toggle and summaries.** "Compare: Source | Pinned" shows while a pin is held; pinning turns Pinned on; Pinned needs a pin. Pinned puts the pin on the left of Side by side and Split and the current encode on the right, each with a caption: `Pinned: <summary> · PSNR x dB vs source` and `Current: <summary> · PSNR x dB vs source`. The summary is format id, colour tag, backend, effort, alpha policy, mip levels and filter; the PSNR is the minimum-mip PSNR shown today, or a dash.
- **AG68 Readout and lens while comparing.** The readout adds `Pinned:` (the pinned encode's texel at the mip on view) and `|Δ| pinned vs encoded`; the strip grows from five to seven lines for as long as the pin is compared, so it does not move while pointing. The lens draws whatever each side draws, so it shows both.
- **AG69 Heatmap vs pin.** With the pin compared, the heatmap is |current − pinned| per channel on the same ramp and full scale, labelled "Heatmap |Δ| Current vs Pinned" (otherwise "Source vs Encoded"). It runs on the same off-thread job; the key gains the pin id, so a new pin or the toggle makes a new job. Like a mip change (AG62), a heatmap made for the other comparison is not shown for this one.
- **AG70 Mip mismatch.** If the pinned encode lacks the mip on view, the left side draws "— not in the pinned encode", the readout's pinned lines read "—", and the heatmap has no image (the plain image shows).
- **AG71 Memory.** The heatmap key holds only the pin id; the job gets the images, and unpinning or pinning again clears the heatmap job state. The summary uses the settings panel words (its format label, the encoder label, the schema names of effort, alpha policy and mips). While the encode on show is not finished the Current caption says "previous result, settings changed". Dropping the pin releases the CPU images (when no one else holds the `Arc`) and `app.rs` clears `Slot::Pinned`. The test checks the CPU side through a `Weak`; the GPU slot needs a device.
- **AG72 3D pan.** Middle-drag or Shift+left-drag pans. render3d's `Camera::Orbit` takes a `target`, so the pan is a GUI-side offset on `Orbit` in bounding radii, added to the bounds centre in `Orbit::camera`. A drag moves `0.002 × distance` per point along the view's right and up vectors (as `View::new` builds them), so the model follows the cursor at any zoom. Double-click, Home and Reset view clear it; a new model resets the orbit as before.
- **AG73 Reset view.** The View section's Reset view now puts the camera back too (lights, background and camera). Home and double-click recentre the camera only.
- **AG74 Sun rotation.** Shift+right-drag turns `Scene::dir_light.direction` about world Z, 0.01 rad per point, kept in 0..2π as `Look::sun_yaw` and saved with the Look (`[layout.look] sun_yaw`, a missing value is 0 and an old gui.toml reads the same). The HDRI and its ambient first stayed fixed; AU4 amends this, they now turn with the sun through `Scene::env_yaw`. Asked for in `nif-request-3d-debug-views.md`.
- **AG75 Debug views.** The selector offers Lit and UV checker only. Lighting.hlsl (CS AIO 2026-10-03) has no debug-view define (only `LLFDEBUG`, a light-count view), and render3d's API has no output switch, so Albedo, Normals, Roughness, Metallic and AO are left out rather than faked; the NIF request lists the API they need. UV checker: a generated 256 px grey 8×8 checker goes over both variants of every shape's slot 0 with `Renderer::replace_texture` after each load, swap and edit (not through the load resolver: the A/B replay would swap the real texture back). Back to Lit reloads the content with its swaps and edits replayed. The view is not saved.
- **AG76 Missing slots in magenta.** The resolver does not make up textures: render3d also sends RFAOS guesses through it, so a magenta answer there would tint every skin. Instead each `LoadReport::missing_textures` path gets a 1×1 magenta DDS with `replace_texture`, in the load's variant only and before the swap replay, so a real swap still wins. A cube slot refuses a 2D texture and keeps render3d's fallback. The panel lists the paths in magenta; a path whose stem matches a Textures item's game path (case-insensitive) has Open in Textures (selects it and switches to the Textures view).
- **AG77 Copy image and keys.** Copy image puts the last frame's RGBA8 on the clipboard with `arboard` (3.6.1, already in the lock through egui-winit, same features); the panel shows "Copied 3D view (W×H)" or the error (the Meshes view has no status bar). The 3D keys (Home, V, Ctrl+Shift+C) are in `ui::actions` as area "3D preview", listed only: the viewports live outside `AppModel`, so `ui::viewport3d` handles them, with the same text-field, dialog and popup guard. The area shares no key with any other, since the set's 3D view is live beside the grid.
- **AG78 The set's 2D | 3D switch works in place (owner, 2026-10-14, from a screenshot of the set editor).** 3D replaces the centre pane's body with the set's 3D view (`state.set3d.v3`) at full width; 2D brings the set editor back. The right-hand `set-3d` side panel is gone, and an old `[layout.widths] set-3d` is ignored. The switch stays at the left of the set editor's header row (name, Input name…), which shows above both views. The 3D view's own controls (Shader folder…, CS toggle, View with the debug views, Copy image) come with it. The TruePBR settings fold in a collapsed "TruePBR settings" section above the view, so a JSON edit still shows live in 3D. AB4 holds: one switch in gui.toml, default 2D, and the preview does no work in 2D. A member texture keeps the view up since AG87.
- **AG79 The Advanced skin card is separate from TruePBR (owner, 2026-10-14).** CS Advanced Skin is set by override JSON files (`Shaders/Skin/Overrides/*.json`), not PGPatcher, and will be patched differently. The set editor shows an "Advanced skin (Community Shaders)" card under the TruePBR card for any set whose diffuse resolves to `skin_albedo` (`SetView::skin`). It is display and per-set editing only: nothing writes a CS override JSON yet (export spec pending), and the header says so. The PGPatcher JSON writer is untouched. Today a skin set still gets the TruePBR card and its PGPatcher entry exactly as before (`is_json_set` is `skin || pbr`); the owner can rule whether that should change once the export spec exists.
- **AG80 Fields.** The 20 per-material `SkinProfile` fields of the CS README section 4, in its order, grouped Specular (5), Physical (4), Edge & detail (5), SSS (3), Fuzz (3). `WetResponse` is wetness, which CS keeps global, so it is not a field. Key names are the exact CS names so the future writer is a straight copy. The list is `model::skin::FIELDS`.
- **AG81 Non-inheritable.** Each field is "not set" (nothing stored; the CS default shows as "default 0.7") or an explicit value on that set. No preset, `[defaults]`, rule, bulk or other-set layer feeds these fields. "set" stores the default value as an explicit one; the usual reset clears it back to not set.
- **AG82 Storage.** The set's `skin` table in `sdc-project.toml` (`skin.F0 = 0.04`), per set, explicit values only. `skin` is a reserved `[[sets]]` key, so it is never a PGPatcher setting and never reaches the JSON editor or writer. `SetEntry::skin` holds every key read, unknown ones included; edits touch only the key changed, so unknown keys survive a save. GUI project data only: no engine change.
- **AG83 Ranges (UI guards only, not CS rules).** DragValue clamp: roughness, F0, strength, Translucency, sssWidth, SkinDetailStrength and the Fuzz fields 0..=1; the multipliers (SkinSpecularTexMultiplier, BaseColorMultiplier, the three Physical*, BodyTilingMultiplier) 0..=4; SkinDetailTiling 0.1..=100.
- **AG84 Undo and bulk.** Each edit is one undo step through `edit_docs`, merged per set and field so a drag is one step, with no replan. Not in bulk: set-level, and Z8 excludes bulk set-level editing.
- **AG85 Skin members.** The card lists the set's `_rfaos`, `_sk` and `_wet` textures (by their resolved sub-types) as text, since CS binds them through its JSON. Nothing there is editable.
- **AG86 Source.** Field names and defaults are from the CS AIO 2026-10-03 `Shaders/Skin/Overrides/README.md`, section 4.
- **AG87 Keep 3D up for a member (owner, 2026-10-14, Task 10 review I1).** With the switch on 3D, selecting any texture of a set (`followed_set`) opens or keeps that set's 3D view in the centre, also when the set itself was never selected (owner, fix round 2). The texture's settings fold in a "<name> settings" section above it, open on first selection and then remembered per texture for the session (egui memory), and an edit there reaches the 3D through the set preview's usual refresh. The 2D | 3D switch shows in that section's header row. A texture of no set shows its normal settings, and the switch stays on 3D. AB4 holds.
- **AG88 3D layout sections.** In 3D the set editor folds the TruePBR settings (the whole `pgp_fields::panel`, so the coat group under Multilayer, Note AJ, comes with it) and, for a skin set, the Advanced skin card above the view. Each scroll area is 45% of the pane's height, at least 240 pt (review minor: 240 pt was cramped). The sections start closed each session, as egui keeps no state across runs.
- **AG89 Skin value rounding.** `set_skin_value` rounds a float to 4 decimals, as the card shows them, so a drag never stores 0.7100000000000001. Tests check every field's key, group, default and range as a table, that a drag on one field is one undo step, and that an unknown `skin.*` key and a comment survive a real folder open, an edit and a save.
- **AG90 Advanced skin sets get no PGPatcher JSON (owner, 2026-10-14: "no need for pgpatcher for advanced skin sets, future export json card").** This replaces final review C1. `SetView::pbr` is now true for a `pbr_albedo` diffuse only, so every PGPatcher reader leaves a `skin_albedo` set out: `is_json_set`, `json_entries` (writer, JSON editor, set preview), `export_blocks`, `export_asks`, prefix ownership, the PGPatcher `rename`, and the output-name rule. `shows_json_panel` is false for a skin set even if it holds old JSON settings, so there is no TruePBR card or prefix line; the badge reads "Advanced skin (no JSON)". The engine side is unchanged: `is_pbr_diffuse` still drives the PBR member follow-ons, and encoding is untouched. An old skin entry leaves the JSON at the next export that writes it, under the usual rules: Overwrite asks when the file was edited outside, and with no PBR set left nothing is written and the old file stays (the existing keep rule). The skin card has a disabled "Export JSON…" placeholder with the hover "Community Shaders Advanced Skin override export - spec pending". The set preview draws a skin set without a JSON entry, as a vanilla set (it would not be PGPatcher-patched in game either). Output location (owner confirmed, fix round 2): with no prefix ownership a skin set's files are written to their normal mirrored paths, not under `textures\pbr\<prefix>\`, and renaming its outputs goes through `output_name`, not the PGPatcher `rename` key. Files an earlier export wrote under `textures\pbr\` stay until the user deletes them; Tessalith deletes nothing.
- **AG91 Plan progress is a planner counter (Task 11, item 13; fix round 1, controller ruling).** The engine planner emits no event while it loads and analyses, and adding one would change the event stream every consumer reads. Instead `BatchRequest::plan_progress: Option<PlanProgress>` carries two `Arc<AtomicUsize>` the GUI hands in per plan: `total` is set to `work.len()` (the files pass 1 loads and resolves; duplicates and skipped DDS inputs are left out) before the pass starts, and `done` goes up once each file's slot is filled (cache hits, load failures and panics included). Nothing is emitted or written: output, events, goldens and the engine version are unchanged, and the CLI passes `None`. Being per request, a cancelled plan still draining never counts into the next one. `spawn_plan`'s ticker thread reads them every 100 ms and sends `PlanProgress { generation, done, total }` when `done` changes; it is stopped and joined before `Planned` is sent, so no progress of a generation follows its result. Pass 2 (packed outputs) is not counted, so the bar can sit full briefly. Bar text: "Analysing 1,234 / 5,000 files…" (comma thousands). This replaces the first version's decode count (`cache().loads()`), which sat at 0 on a warm re-open.
- **AG92 Cancel an open.** `cancel_open` cancels the plan's token and bumps the generation, so its `Planned` (a cancelled plan still returns `Ok`) is dropped like any stale result (the generation rule `replan` and audit Task 12 use). `open_folder` clears the previous folder's state before planning, so "previous folder intact" is done by opening that folder again (controller ruling: deferring the clears would touch every path that reads `root`, `project` or `sets_doc`). Its analyses are normally still in the session cache (a cancelled plan never runs `retain_plan`), so it re-plans quickly. Selection, undo history and its export drawer start fresh. With no previous folder the window goes empty (`root`, project and sets doc cleared) and the centre reads "Opening cancelled". Going back is not itself cancellable.
- **AG93 Cancel only while opening; Recent; hover.** The bar and Cancel show while a folder's first plan is in flight (`can_cancel_open`), not for a re-plan after an edit (that keeps the plain status text): a cancelled re-plan would leave the grid showing a plan that no longer matches the project. A second open during an open keeps the first open's previous folder as the one Cancel returns to. Cancel puts Recent back as it was before the open, so a cancelled folder new to Recent is dropped and one already listed keeps its place. Cancel's hover: "Stops opening and re-opens the previous folder (selection and undo history are reset)", or "Stops opening; no folder stays open" when there is none.
- **AG94 No streaming thumbnails (AG7).** The grid's items are not known until the plan lands: packed outputs come from pass 2, DDS skips and duplicates are decided across files, and the Status sort (AG4) needs each file's warnings. Placeholders in final sort order are therefore impossible before `Planned`, and the planner has no per-file result to stream without an engine change. Progress bar only. Editing stays gated on `busy()` (planning counts) as before.
- **AG95 Scan row bar and ETA.** The scan row's `phase done/total` becomes a progress bar. The ETA reuses Task 5's `Pace` (10 s window, no ETA before 3 items or 2 s): each newly done item is one `finished`, and a new phase or a count that went back starts a fresh `Pace`. The app already repaints every 50 ms while a plan or a scan runs; nothing else polls.
- **AG96 Used by meshes (Task 12, item 9).** The settings panel's collapsed "Used by meshes (N)" list is read per selected texture, off the UI thread (`used_by_for`, a worker thread, `WorkerMsg::UsedBy` tagged with a generation; an answer for an older ask is dropped). It is not part of the mesh-evidence read: that read gates `finish_plan`, and a per-texture list would slow every plan. The key is `mesh_key(game_path, output stem) + ".dds"`, as the mesh votes use. One row per (NIF, mod) whose shapes use the texture in any slot, across enabled origins, winner first: loose before BSA (`kind = 4`, whose priority is the archive rank), then MO2 priority descending, then path. Capped at 200 with "and N more". "decided alpha" marks rows only when the texture's `alpha.policy` provenance is `mesh:alpha`, and then follows `mesh_alpha_policy` over the slot-0 source lighting shapes (not TruePBR, not derived): every row with a blending shape for blend; every row with a shape testing at the threshold used for cutout; both for cutout + blend; none for "remove", which all shapes give alike. The list is read again after a scan finishes (the old one stays until the new one lands) and dropped on folder open. Raw SQL on `Store::conn()`, as `mesh_alpha` (modscan is NIF-owned).
- **AG97 Open in Textures (item 9).** Every texture path the Meshes view lists (the shape cards' slots and the "Users of" title) gets "Open in Textures" when `item_for_game_path` finds it in the open folder. `SdcApp::route_3d` looks the paths up again only when they or the items change, and runs the click as the 3D panel's "Show in Textures" does (switch view, `palette_pick`: select and scroll into view). The 3D panel's missing-texture button uses the same name (it was "Show in Textures" until Task 13). The Plugin file references window is not covered: its Missing paths are in no mod by definition, and its Unused lines are listing text.
- **AG98 Drop files on a slot (item 17).** egui-winit 0.36 / winit 0.30.13 report a file drop with no position, and no mouse moves reach the window during an OLE drag, so the drop point is read with `GetCursorPos` (windows-sys feature `Win32_UI_WindowsAndMessaging` added to the existing dependency, no new crate) and placed with the viewport's inner rect; without viewport info (tests) the last pointer position stands in. The Textures view handles its drops in `ui::draw_with_scan`: a slot card under the point takes them, and anything else falls through to the old "open the folder" after the frame. The file must be an item of the open folder whose slot (`slot_of`) is that card's; another slot or none is refused with the reason. It then goes through `drop_choice` with Move, so a filled slot asks Replace / Cancel (the same prompt, now drawn from `ui::draw_with_scan` so a hidden Sets column still shows it) and a locked slot refuses. Share and Duplicate are never offered from a file drop, so nothing is copied. Refusals and "only the first file was used" show as a line under the slot cards. In 3D the cards are not drawn, so a drop opens the folder.
- **AG99 Fill from names (item 18).** In the single-set menu (hand-drawn, after Move down). It is per set, so it is not in the multi-set `set_menu` list nor in `ui::actions`. Candidates are folder items (not a disk listing) in the diffuse member's folder (else the set id's) that are not members and not packed outputs, whose stem's `base_of` (the engine's `split_vocab` then `split_suffix`, so `SUFFIXES` and the exporter vocabulary, not duplicated) equals the diffuse's base (else the id's last segment) with a tail, and whose sub-type fills a slot card. A locked slot reads "locked" and cannot be ticked; a second file for one slot, a slot 3 or 7 clash, a packed occupant, or leaving a locked slot elsewhere block the row with their reason. Apply re-reads the proposals and runs one `edit_docs` (one undo step, one plan) that empties each slot and adds the member. Esc cancels, Enter applies, a click outside does nothing (AG11). With no proposal at all, a refusal line shows instead of an empty dialog.
- **AG100 Moving a file back into the set that removed it.** `SetsDoc::add_member` now lifts that set's own `remove` for the file instead of adding an `add` beside it, which left the file out. Found by Fill from names; the Sets column's move had the same gap.
- **AG101 Subsurface mode selector (Task 12b, owner 2026-10-14).** The TruePBR card's Subsurface checkbox and the hidden "Foliage subsurface" checkbox become one selector, Subsurface: Off / Standard / Foliage. Standard writes `subsurface: true`, Foliage writes `subsurface_foliage: true`, Off writes neither; it never writes both and never a `false` (OnlyTrue stays). Why two kinds exist: the schema says `subsurface` "sets or clears the Rim_Lighting flag that enables subsurface scattering. true also puts <prefix>_s.dds in slot 8" and `subsurface_foliage` "true puts <prefix>_s.dds in slot 8 for foliage subsurface scattering" (no flag). Community Shaders `Common/PBR.hlsli:174-187` runs both under the PBR Subsurface flag and picks the term at compile time by `TREE_ANIM` (SLSF2 bit 29): other meshes get forward and back scatter, Tree_Anim meshes get `saturate(-NdotL) * (1 - Thickness)`. The owner's load order sets `subsurface_foliage: false` in 7,383 default-block entries and `true` in none. The "Subsurface settings" group (colour, opacity) shows for Standard and for Foliage (both read `SubsurfaceColor` and `Thickness`); hair and multilayer still rule both out. Both keys stored (a hand edit or the JSON editor) shows "Standard + Foliage (conflict)" with a pitfall and is never rewritten; choosing a mode replaces both keys. Off on a set with an `_s` stores `subsurface: false` so auto stays off (AJ8); on a set without one it removes both keys. A Foliage set with no `_s` blocks export like Standard does.
- **AG102 Subsurface auto mode (extends AJ).** With an `_s` member and Multilayer off (with it on `_s` is `coat_diffuse`, AJ), auto picks Foliage when the set's scanned meshes are Tree_Anim, Foliage at low confidence when there is no scan and the set's path holds `textures\trees\`, `\landscape\trees\` or `\plants\` (covers `\landscape\plants\`; the check adds a `textures\` lead to a derived match that lacks one), else Standard. The why reads "Foliage: the set's meshes use Tree Anim" or "Foliage: path under textures\trees (no scan)". A scan that found shapes and none Tree_Anim beats the path hint (Standard), so an auto value never trips the AG104 pitfall; this narrows the brief's "otherwise Foliage on a path hint". Any explicit `subsurface` or `subsurface_foliage` value, `false` included, wins.
- **AG103 Tree_Anim evidence reuses the mesh votes read.** `meshes::mesh_tree_anim` runs on the same worker, over the same keys (`shader::colour_mesh_key` of the colour-member jobs) and the same slot-0 source lighting shapes (`SLOT0_SOURCE_SHAPES`) as `mesh_votes` and `mesh_alpha`, reading `shape.slsf2` bit 29. A key is Tree_Anim when any of its shapes is; a key with no shape has no entry. The result is `AppModel::mesh_tree_anim`; it is not sent to the engine and does not trigger a replan. A set's answer is its diffuse member's key. No SQL runs on the UI thread. The evidence is only as fresh as the last plan's read.
- **AG104 Subsurface pitfalls.** `prefix::subsurface_pitfalls`, in the export "Proceed anyway" list: both keys on (needs no scan); Foliage on a set whose scanned meshes are not Tree_Anim, "Foliage subsurface only shades on Tree Anim meshes."; Standard on a set whose scanned meshes are Tree_Anim, "Standard subsurface sets Rim_Lighting on a Tree Anim mesh; Foliage is likely meant.". With no scan neither of those two fires. The card shows the same text in amber under the selector. They read the set's own settings, so auto values never trigger them. The JSON editor keeps listing both raw keys (`KEYS`); Bulk does not cover set-level settings (Z8).
- **AG105 Task 12 review carry-overs.** `layout::window_now` multiplies the viewport rectangles by `ctx.zoom_factor()` (`native_points`) before saving, so a window closed at egui zoom other than 1 comes back at the same size and place. A selector or field edit that writes several keys of one set (`AppModel::set_json_values`, used by the TruePBR panel) is one `batch_edit`: one undo step, one plan; a single key keeps the merge of `set_json_value`. The 3D panel button is "Open in Textures". Skipped Minors: the per-frame `is_file` stat and `listed_textures` Vec in `used_by_for`/`route_3d`, the stale "decided alpha" mark until reselect, and the stale cursor fallback of `drop_pos` (tests and non-Windows only).
- **AG106 Issues on entries (Task 13, item 11).** `pbr_export::json_issues` sorts `export_blocks` (errors, red) and `export_asks` (warnings, amber) by set id onto the editor's entries, with the "<id>: " prefix dropped. An issue with no set id, or about a TruePBR set that has no entry (it matches nothing), goes in the General box at the top; an issue about a set that is not a TruePBR set (an Advanced skin set, AG90) is dropped, so skin sets never appear or fail here. Delete-section hits keep their own amber lines and are not counted.
- **AG107 Header and navigation.** The header reads "N errors, M warnings" (singular at 1). Next error / Prev walk the entries that have an error (General first when it has one), wrapping; the position is the last entry jumped to. A jump unfolds the entry and scrolls it into view. Entries got a Collapse / Expand button for this (default open). F8 and Shift+F8 are listed in `ui::actions` under the new area "JSON editor" (never live in the main window: the editor is a window of its own and handles them, as the 3D panel does); the area shares no key with another.
- **AG108 Search.** A case-insensitive substring of the set id or label, or of any path in the entry (matches including the derived match1, slot paths and commands, path_contains, nif_filter). "showing K of N". The counts and Next error work on all entries; a target the search hides clears the search.
- **AG109 Text and issues cache.** The editor builds its entries, JSON text and issues once per `AppModel::json_key` (the set-view key: items, inputs, sets-document revision, root, export root, target; plus the mesh evidence count and the JSON name) and keeps them in egui's per-window memory. `json_editor::cache_builds` counts the rebuilds for the test. Shortcut: a change the key does not see (the mesh evidence changing without its count changing) shows after the next edit.
- **AG110 Pinning the auto subsurface mode (Task 12b review).** Choosing the selector mode that equals the auto value now stores an explicit value (Standard `subsurface: true`, Foliage `subsurface_foliage: true`, Off as AG101), so a later scan or path change cannot move it. A hand-set mode that equals the choice still does nothing. The mesh-evidence read no longer fails as a whole when only the Tree_Anim query fails: that evidence is left empty. Skipped minors: the `\plants\` path hint stays as is, and the mixed Tree_Anim set rule is documented behaviour.
- **AG111 Process-list seam (Task 14 carry-over).** The "PGPatcher is running" check is `MeshesModel::procs`, a fn pointer that defaults to the real process list. `meshes::no_procs` is the fake that sees nothing; the meshes tests build their scan models with it, so the host's PGPatcher cannot pause them, and `a_running_pgpatcher_pauses_the_scan` uses a fake that lists `pgpatcher.exe`. Not seamed: the NVTT probe reads the host's `PATH` and install, but only when a test calls `refresh_nvtt`, and those tests set `nvtt_path`.
- **AG112 Toasts.** A toast is a short notice at the bottom right that never takes input. It stays 4 s (8 s for a warning or error) and fades over its last 0.5 s. At most 3 are on screen; later ones queue and start their time when they appear. The pointer over a toast freezes its time. The clock is a parameter of `Messages::tick`, so the tests drive it. Raised by: Saved (a save that wrote a file), Export finished (N written, M skipped, K failed; a warning when K > 0), Scan finished (the scan's summary; a paused or cancelled scan repeats its note as a warning), Copied 3D view / Copy failed, Picker result ignored, and the Undid / Redid note. What needs a decision (Proceed anyway, Replace / Cancel, an external change, a blocking error) stays a dialog; no event changed its error class.
- **AG113 One store.** `model::messages::Messages` holds the log and the toasts; the status-line notes call `log` (encoding-follows-type, Export cancelled, Ctrl+S leave the field) and the routine results call `notify` (log and toast). The status line itself is unchanged. The log keeps the last 500 entries, each with local time (`GetLocalTime`; UTC off Windows, a shortcut), severity and text, in memory only.
- **AG114 Messages panel.** A window listing the entries newest first with a Clear button. It opens from the header's Messages button, which shows the unread count (entries logged while the panel was closed), and from the action `messages` (Ctrl+M, area Global, so it is in the palette, the F1 sheet and the docs table). An open panel reads entries as they arrive.
- **AG115 RNM blended preview.** `ui::rnm_section` shows a 128 px thumbnail (longer side) of the normal with its enabled layers blended in, drawn as `(n + 1) / 2`. It uses the engine blend `rnm::blend::apply` (already `pub`; no engine edit) on the base after `ops::preprocess`, with the base downscaled by `blend::resample` first. Layers load at their own size and are resampled to the thumbnail by the engine. It is a small-size look, not the final pixels (the base is downscaled before the blend; the export blends at full size).
- **AG116 Preview job.** The heatmap pattern (`model::rnm_preview`): one job in flight; the picture on show stays until the one for the new key lands. The key is the item, the base file and stamp, the layers (files, stamps) and the profile `rnm` and `channels`. A result is compared with the item key as it is when the result lands, and dropped when they differ; the next frame then starts the current one. A failed blend shows "Preview unavailable".
- **AG117 Refresh.** The strength slider only writes on release, and the order only changes with the arrows; each makes a new plan with a new key, so the preview refreshes exactly then.
- **AG118 Output size line.** Each texture settings panel shows "Output: FORMAT · W×H · N mips · SIZE ≈ VRAM" from the engine rules: `resize::target_dims`, `mips::level_count`, `DdsPixelFormat::level_size` per level, `dds::resolve_header` for 128 or 148 header bytes. The hover gives the exact bytes. A BC7 2048² with a full chain is 5,592,432 bytes in 12 levels (the brief figure of 5,592,416 is the same chain stopped at 2x2).
- **AG119 Totals.** The status line under the header shows "≈ VRAM: selection X · folder Y" and the bulk panel the same. The folder is every texture whose job is Ready or NeedsDecision (written, or once decided); skipped, failed, packed-into and DDS inputs are left out. "≈ VRAM" because compressed size equals VRAM for BC formats; the totals are file sizes with headers.
- **AG120 Size cache and header reads (fix round 1).** Sizes are kept per `items_rev` (`SizeCache`), the folder total too; nothing reads a file on the UI thread. The source size comes first from the planner's own analysis (`AnalysisCache::dims(path, stamp)`, a read-only engine accessor; no engine output changes). Files the planner did not analyse (a packed output's source, and the like) are read from their headers (`image::image_dimensions`; the DDS header by hand) by one worker at a time (`WorkerMsg::Dims`, the heatmap pattern), once per file and plan stamp. Until it lands the totals say "(+N unsized)", then they are rebuilt. A PSD or an unreadable file stays unsized (shortcut: read the PSD header if it matters). Tests count header reads on the UI thread through `out_size::header_reads_on_this_thread`.
- **AG120a Toast queue.** A new toast replaces a waiting one of the same kind (the text before the first colon: "Undid", "Saved", "Export finished"), and the queue holds at most 5 (the oldest go). The log keeps every entry. The Ctrl+S note is logged once per key press, not per key repeat.
- **AG121 Per-key drag.** Every number key has a `DragSpec { speed, decimals, log }` in the `KEYS` table (`pgp/schema.rs`), taken from the field research (`pgp-numeric-ranges.md`, 1393 PGPatcher JSONs). It is applied by `pgp_fields::control` (so the TruePBR card and the JSON editor) and by the NiAlphaProperty threshold box. Only the drag feel and the shown precision change: the NumRange clamps and the stored values do not, and the integer keys keep whole-number steps. A hand-set value with more places than `decimals` shows rounded (`glint.density_randomization` 4.525 shows as 5) and stays as stored until edited.
- **AG122 Log keys.** egui 0.36's `DragValue` has no `logarithmic`, so a `log` key uses the value-scaled speed: `max(speed, |value| * 0.01)` (`DragSpec::speed_at`). `uv_scale`, `emissive_scale`, `displacement_scale`, `specular_level`, `coat_specular_level`, `inner_uv_scale`, `glint.microfacet_roughness` and `glint.density_randomization` are log. Shortcut: no field data for `auto_uv`, `uv_scale`, `inner_uv_scale`, `alphaprop_flags` and `alphaprop_thresh` (zero uses in the 1393 files); their specs are the report's guesses, to retune when seen. `alphaprop_flags` steps by 16 per pixel; the hex box and the flag checkboxes are the real editors.
- **AG123 Glint defaults.** The glint starting values were checked against the observed p1-p99 band (`pgp-numeric-stats.csv`); one outside it moved to the observed median, rounded: `log_microfacet_density` 40 to 10 (observed 6-24, median 12.9, 10 is the second most common value, 12 uses; owner ruling), `microfacet_roughness` 0.015 to 1 (band 0.063-1.43, median 1, 31 of 58 uses), `density_randomization` 2 to 5 (band 2.128-300, median 5, 32 of 58 uses). `screen_space_scale` 1.5 stays (band 1-4). Fuzz has no default; `weight` is written only when set (superseded by AG133: fuzz now has defaults). The fixed `default` block (`roughness_scale` 1, `specular_level` 0.04, `displacement_scale` 1) already sits on the observed medians. An old project that switched glint on and never touched a value exports the new defaults.
- **AG124 The black bar between the columns (owner, 2026-10-14, "still").** Cause: a column's content ran wider than the width the column was given. egui's `Panel` clips the content and its own fill to the given width but moves the next panel, the divider and the stored size to the content's edge, and `column_panel` re-applies the chosen width every frame, so the strip between the two was never painted and showed eframe's near-black clear colour. Two widgets did it: the Textures filter combos (`ComboBox::width` is a minimum, and a truncated combo takes the whole rest of the row, so a row of them overran the column; the old `COMBO_PAD` guess was 12 px short), and the Sets column's shader combo (in a `horizontal_wrapped` row `available_width` is the row's, not what is left after the badges, so it never wrapped). Fix: each filter combo gets an exact share of its row, and the shader combo measures the room left in the row. The collapsed strips (a column's and the Advanced one) had no fill either; they now use the panel fill, and the window clear colour is the panel fill too, so no gap can ever show black. Tests paint-probe a row across the window (every opaque fill, within its clip) at a sweep of column widths, mid-drag with files hovering, and with columns collapsed or hidden.
- **AG125 Path-picker popups stay open while used (owner, 2026-10-14).** egui menus default to `PopupCloseBehavior::CloseOnClick`, so a click in the "Read this set's textures as" filter box closed the popup. `path_picker::menu` opens a menu with `CloseOnClickOutside`; *Input name…* and the JSON editor's two ▾ pickers (match path, mesh path) use it. A pick closes it explicitly (`ui.close()`, as before), and so do Esc and a click outside. Not changed: menus of buttons only ("+ key", "+", Recent, the set and member menus) and the combo boxes, which hold neither a text field nor a scrolled list; the palette and the dialogs are modals, not menus.
- **AG126 The header wraps (owner, 2026-10-14: the header compresses and the JSON field overlaps).** Cause: the header was one `horizontal` row with a `right_to_left` group (Export, Save, Messages, Export folder, JSON name, Editor…) laid into whatever width the left group left. egui does not stop a group at its neighbour, so in a narrow window the two groups drew over each other and the JSON field was squeezed to a few pixels. Ruling: of the two options in the brief, `horizontal_wrapped` is the simpler correct one, so nothing is hidden behind a "⋯" menu. The row is one left-to-right wrapped line in this order: Open folder…, Recent, Undo, Redo, the view switch, Mode, the column checkboxes, JSON: [name] Editor…, Export folder, Messages, Save, Export. Groups egui cannot wrap by themselves (Recent's disabled scope, Mode and its combo, the JSON label + field + Editor…) start a new line when they would not fit (`header::fit`). The JSON field is always 120 points; the export folder button is at most 280 points, the path cut with "…" (the hover and the accessible name keep it whole). Export is now at the end of the row, not pinned right. The Meshes header (the view switch) and the scan row were already wrapped and are covered by the same test: at 1440, 1024, 800 and 640 points no two header widgets intersect, none runs off the window, and every control is present.
- **AG127 A panicked size job (final review M3).** The header worker's files are kept as in flight. If the job panics, each file not yet retried leaves the asked set, so the next plan (a new `items_rev`) asks for it again; a file whose retry panics too is stored as unsized (`None`) for the session, and the Messages log gets one warning line with the count. The "internal error in dims" dialog is unchanged. `SizeCache::reader` is the test seam (`None` = `source_dims`).
- **AG128 Used by meshes: panics, stats and key repeat (final review M4, M5, M6).**
  - **M4:** a Used-by read that panics clears `pending` and shows "Reading the scan database failed: <message>" in the list for that texture (as a read error does); the "internal error" dialog is unchanged. It is not read again until a scan finishes or another texture is asked for.
  - **M5:** the scan database check in `used_by_for` and Show in Explorer's per-member check (`reveal_problem`, the set menu and the bulk menu) go through `AppModel::stats`, the drawer's `StatCache` (one stat per path per second). The click itself (`reveal`) still checks the disk.
  - **M6:** of the two options, the 150 ms settle (`USED_BY_SETTLE`): a new texture bumps the generation (a read in flight for the old one is dropped when it lands) and its read starts once it has stayed 150 ms. Asking again for the same texture after a scan reads at once. Holding Up or Down starts no read until the key is released. The app repaints while one waits or runs (`used_by_pending`): a read in flight, or a wanted texture that is neither being read nor read yet, for as long as a draw keeps asking for it (500 ms after the last ask), so a settle cannot be missed. A folder open forgets the wanted texture, and one nobody draws any more (a set selected) stops the frames.
- **AG129 Folder and export state (final review M7, M8, M9, M11).**
  - **M7:** the header's export folder button is disabled while an export runs, hover "An export is running: change the folder after it ends", so the drawer's Open export folder and Copy CLI command keep pointing at the folder the rows were written to. An export folder dialog opened before the export started and answered during it is dropped with the usual "Picker result ignored" note and toast.
  - **M8:** `cancel_open` with no previous folder clears `export_after_plan` and `continue_after_plan` (`AppModel::export_queued` reads them).
  - **M9:** `open_folder` clears `SizeCache` (`clear_folder`: sizes, asked, retried and pending files), so it holds one folder's sizes, not the session's.
  - **M11 (amends AG42):** OpenFolder is no longer folder-scoped. The folder picked is the user's latest explicit choice, so it opens (through `request_open` and its unsaved-edits and export-running dialogs) even when another folder was opened or the open was cancelled while the dialog was up. ExportFolder and SaveReport stay folder-scoped.
- **AG130 One format-name table, and the report written off the UI thread (final review M10, M12).**
  - **M10:** `fields::format_name` ("BC7", "BC6H", "RGBA16F") is the one name table; `fields::format_label` builds "BC7 · sRGB" from it, and the output size line uses it. `out_size::format_name` (the serde id upper-cased, which read "BC6H_UF16") is gone.
  - **M12:** Save report builds the text on the UI thread (it reads the export model) and writes the file on a worker (`AppModel::save_report`, `WorkerMsg::ReportSaved`). The result goes to the status line and a toast: "Saved report: <path>", or "Could not write the report to <path>: <error>" as an error toast. A failed write no longer raises a dialog. `drawer::write_report` stays as the synchronous form for tests.
- **AG132 Compact 3D layout (owner, 2026-10-15: "TruePBR settings ... an optional pop-out window, with all of the tech stuff collapsible at the bottom").** Amends AG78 and AG87.
  - **Settings window.** The inline "TruePBR settings" and "Advanced skin settings" sections of the 3D layout, and AG87's folded "<name> settings" section, are gone. **TruePBR settings…** (a toggle in the 3D header row, beside Input name… or the texture's name) opens one `egui::Window` (`set_preview::settings_window`, a fixed id so its place carries over): a selected member's own settings panel, then the followed set's TruePBR panel (coat group, NiAlphaProperty) and the skin card. The title is "TruePBR settings" for a set and "<name> settings" for a member. Edits go through the same model calls, so the 3D follows them live. It shows only while the centre shows the 3D view (not over an expanded preview, the bulk panel or 2D, which keep the flag). It follows the selection; a selection in 3D with neither member settings, a JSON panel nor a skin card clears the flag. Open: `GuiSettings::set_settings_open`, saved at once like the 2D | 3D switch. Place and size: `[layout] settings_window = [x, y, w, h]`, read from egui's area memory by `layout::capture` and given back with `default_rect` (so it rides the 2 s layout debounce; sanitised, a bad value is dropped).
  - **The 3D view fills the space.** `Panel::top` (toolbar line + warnings) and `Panel::bottom` (Details) inside the centre, the frame in the rest; nothing reserves a fixed height any more (`BELOW` is gone). Toolbar: Encoded | Source, the mesh combo (its text now "Mesh: Auto (Sphere)"), **View ▾** (the old View section as a menu that stays open while used, AG125's `path_picker::menu`), Copy image, Shader folder…. The heading "Set preview (3D)" (`PANEL_TITLE`, removed) and the second path line are dropped.
  - **Warnings stay visible.** Above the frame: the viewport notice, Community Shaders not found or failed, locating, LE mesh, render and compile errors, failed shaders, missing textures (magenta, Open in Textures; the list scrolls past 100 points), NEEDS_CS, waits for export, the mesh query error, a failed / stale / blocked map, a skipped shape or a badge (scrolls past 80 points), and the panel notices.
  - **Details** (collapsed by default): JSON_WINS, the preview size, the mesh line, the other maps' states and times, the compiling and plain shape lines, the emissive note, Timings, the Community Shaders features toggle, the P0 off list, the off line, coverage, frame stats, adapter and the renderer's notes. It scrolls past 220 points. Its open state is per panel, in egui memory, and saved as `[layout] details = ["set-3d-details", "meshes-3d-details"]`.
  - **Meshes view.** Its 3D panel uses the same toolbar, warnings, frame and Details footer (`viewport3d::show` composes `toolbar`, `warnings`, `image`, `details`).
- **AG133 Fuzz defaults and float colours (owner, 2026-10-15: "fuzz color should start at .04 .04 .04 or .08 .08 .08, and fuzz strength when enabled should be 1"; RGB fields must allow values above 1.0).** Amends AG2, AG9 and AG123.
  - **Evidence:** in the owner's mod JSONs `fuzz.color` is [0.08, 0.08, 0.08] 231 times, [0.04, 0.04, 0.04] 110 times and [1.25, 1.25, 1.25] 39 times.
  - **Fuzz defaults:** `fuzz.color` [0.08, 0.08, 0.08] and `fuzz.weight` 1.0 (`pgp::settings::FUZZ_COLOR_DEFAULT`, `FUZZ_WEIGHT_DEFAULT`; `number_default` and `rgb_default` are the one lookup for the card, the JSON editor's + menu and the writer). Like glint, an unset value of an enabled Fuzz shows the default greyed and the writer writes it; nothing is stored until it is edited, and an explicit value is kept. An old project with Fuzz on and no weight now exports weight 1, and one with no colour set now exports `color` [0.08, 0.08, 0.08] too (before, no colour was written).
  - **Storage:** a colour (`emissive_color`, `subsurface_color`, `coat_color`, `fuzz.color`, every Rgb/Rgba key) is stored as one float per channel, 0 to `RGB_MAX` (10). The document keeps JSON numbers as they are (a TOML float stays a float), so 0.08 is stored exactly.
  - **Old projects:** a colour whose channels are all integers is the 0-255 form of earlier versions. It reads as value/255 with no loss (`settings::rgb_of`), stays in the file as it is until edited (no rewrite on load, so opening a project marks nothing dirty), and the writer keeps its old output for it (value/255 rounded to 4 places), so an untouched old project exports the same JSON. The first edit stores floats.
