  - **The row:** swatch and `#RRGGBB` box (AG2) show the colour clamped to 1.0; a change through them writes 0-1 values; opening the colour square and closing it writes nothing, so a stored 1.25 survives. Then R, G, B DragValues, one float per channel, 3 decimals, speed 0.005, range 0-10.
  - **Output:** a float colour is written as stored (clamped to 0-10), so 0.08 stays 0.08 and 1.25 stays 1.25.

## Note AH — Complex `_m` alpha and Enable parallax (2026-10-14)

- **AH1 Complex `_m` without source alpha writes alpha 0 (owner, 2026-10-14; widens Note M1).**
  - **Before:** M1/P24 set the 0 default only for a packed `cpm` `_m` height. A loose `_m` typed `complex_material` whose source has no alpha channel resolved `alpha.policy = remove`, from the analysis `suggest_alpha` "source has no alpha channel". It was written with a synthesised 255, which CS reads as a plain env mask (smallest-mip alpha >= 251/255, Spike S8).
  - **Now:** `suggest_alpha` gives `data` for `complex_material` with no alpha, with the reason "source has no alpha channel: alpha 0 (complex material on, parallax off)". The pipeline fills alpha with 0 before Preprocess (`ops::complex_alpha_zero`), so every mip is 0. The fill is 0, not 128: mid values run parallax on a flat height and pick up compression noise.
  - **BC7 exactness:** bc7e can encode a constant alpha-0 block as mode 6 with p-bit 1, which decodes alpha 1. So `encoders_sys::bc7_fixup::force_zero_alpha` runs after the encode, on both the bc7e and the NVTT paths. It applies to a `complex_material` with `alpha.parallax` not on, and only to blocks whose source alpha is 0 throughout.
    - It clears the alpha endpoints and, in modes 6 and 7, the p-bits they share with RGB.
    - RGB endpoints that had p-bit 1 move down, unoptimised, like the S5b mode-6 opaque patch: one LSB (1/255) in mode 6, one step of the 6-bit value (about 4/255) in mode 7. Modes 4 and 5 lose nothing.
    - BC3's alpha block stores 0 exactly.
    - Parallax on keeps a source that has alpha byte for byte. So the warned parallax-on-without-alpha state (AH2) may decode 1, which is still <= 4.
  - **`exact_opaque` on `complex_material`:** kept `true`, because it still matters for an `_m` with alpha whose flat regions are 255. It is irrelevant for a 0 fill, so `ALPHA-EXACT-OFF-M` is not raised for an alpha-0 `_m`, even with `exact_opaque` off.
  - **Warnings:** the `SUG-CONTRADICTION` "reads as a plain envmask" fires only when a solid 255 is actually written (parallax on). `CHAN-CONSTANT-ALPHA-DATA` is not raised for a 0 fill. `VAL-M-GRAYSCALE` (grey, non-black RGB) still applies.
  - **Output identity:** the engine version stays 0.7.0 (owner rule). The `profile_sha256` of every `complex_material` changes once, because of AH2's new field, so `--on-existing if-changed` re-encodes them.
- **AH2 "Enable parallax" on the complex `_m` (owner, 2026-10-14: "a button labeled enable parallax (off sets alpha to 0) on the cpm card").**
  - **Field:** new per-file `alpha.parallax` (`Option<bool>`). It is serialised only when set, so only `complex_material` profiles change.
    - Off: the output alpha is 0 at every mip, whatever the source alpha.
    - On: the source alpha is kept as height (`data`).
  - **Default (fix round 1):** resolved at the analysis position, below every explicit layer, so `[files]`, rules and `--set` win.
    - On only when the source alpha really varies: analysis `Varying` and alpha min < max (`analysis:varying-alpha`).
    - Off for any constant alpha (0, 128 or 255) and for Note L's near-opaque noise (`analysis:constant-alpha`).
    - Off for no alpha (`analysis:no-alpha`).
  - **Solid 255:** a solid-255 `_m` typed `complex_material` writes 0 unless parallax is turned on. With parallax on, 255 stays and raises `SUG-CONTRADICTION` and `VAL-M-COMPLEX-MARGIN`.
  - **No source alpha:** parallax on is not available. It raises `ALPHA-PARALLAX-NO-ALPHA` (warning), and the output stays 0.
  - **Packed cpm `_m`:** the default follows the same rule, read from the assembled image. With no height source, the alpha is the constant M1 default 0, so parallax reads off. A `packing.fill.height` is a constant alpha too (fix round 2, coordinator ruling): parallax stays off and the alpha is 0, because the owner rejected a flat height such as 128. To write the fill, turn "Enable parallax" on (`alpha.parallax = true`). The checks see that final value, so a 255 fill with parallax on and `exact_opaque` off on BC7 raises `ALPHA-EXACT-OFF-M`.
  - **Explicit `alpha.policy = remove`:** wins, and 255 is synthesised. The parallax row is hidden ("alpha removed: 255"), and `ALPHA-PARALLAX-NO-ALPHA` is not raised.
  - **GUI:**
    - An "Enable parallax" row in the Alpha group, for complex material only.
    - The same box under the set editor's slot cards, for a packed or loose complex `_m`, because it has no slot card.
    - Hover text: "Off writes alpha 0: complex material on, parallax off". The box is greyed out, with the reason, when the source has no alpha.
    - A ↺ next to the box clears the texture's own value, because a locked, ticked box cannot be unticked.
    - Each toggle is one `set_field` edit: one undo step and a replan.
  - **Bulk:** picks the row up from `FIELDS`. A hidden field's skip reason now comes from `fields::hidden_reason`, one reason per field. It used to say "cutout only" for every hidden field.
  - **Tests changed:** the golden `exact_opaque_m_decodes_alpha_255_at_every_mip` gains `alpha.parallax=true` on its solid-255 `iron_m`, so it still tests the 255 path. `policy_checks::contradictions_are_warnings` gets the same addition.

## Note AI — Meshes shape detail (2026-10-14)

- **AI1 The Shapes toggle moves the left column, not the detail pane (owner, 2026-10-14; replaces the Shapes | 3D selector).** A "Shapes" selectable button next to the "Mods and meshes" title swaps that column to the selected mesh's shape cards; off shows the mods and meshes list. The detail pane always draws 3D. The button is disabled with "Select a mesh first" when no mesh is selected, and the column falls back to the list if the selection clears. The state is `MeshesModel::shapes_col` (replaces `tab_3d`); neither was ever saved to `gui.toml`.
- **AI2 Shape numbers count shapes from 0 (owner).** The `#N` is the index in the shape list the GUI holds (the scan stores shapes only, ordered by NIF block), so bones and other non-shapes are skipped. The NIF block shows in the hover text. pgpsim has no shape index of its own (it matches by `nif_filter` on the path), so there was no order to match. Whether PGPatcher's texture-set order is block order or a tree walk is not confirmed; see `.superpowers/sdd/meshes/nif-request-shape-index.md`. **Superseded by AU6:** the card number is PGPatcher's 3D index.
- **AI3 Shader line.** No "Lighting:" prefix; the shader type name is bold. Effect and other shaders are bold the same way.
- **AI4 SLSF1/SLSF2 expanders.** Collapsed headers "SLSF1 0x…" and "SLSF2 0x…"; open lists the set bits by name, lowest first, `Name (bit)`, and an unknown bit as `bit N`. The nif crate exposes only the few bits it uses, so the names are GUI-side tables (`SLSF1_NAMES`, `SLSF2_NAMES`, the nif.xml names). SLSF2 bit 23 reads "Unused01 (TruePBR)". Open state is egui memory, per shape.
- **AI5 Alpha in words.** "Alpha: blend <src> → <dst> · test <func>, threshold N", decoded by `pgp::alpha::AlphaFlags` (no second decoder); blend or test is omitted when off, "Alpha: none" when both are. The raw value stays in the hover text.
- **AI6 Slot rows are indented** one `ui.indent` level under the shape's header lines.

## Note AJ — Coat settings under Multilayer (2026-10-14)

- **AJ1. The coat settings are a group under the Multilayer (coat) checkbox, not in Advanced.** The group holds coat colour, coat specular level, coat roughness, coat strength, coat diffuse, coat normal, coat parallax and inner UV scale, in schema key order. It shows only while Multilayer is on. `coat_color` and `inner_uv_scale` were the only two marked advanced; the schema flag is now off, and Multilayer is no longer an Advanced group.
- **AJ2. The switch is Multilayer, not coat diffuse.** The owner asked for the group under "coat diffuse". Evidence: the PGPatcher schema says `multilayer` is what makes the `coat_*` keys and `inner_uv_scale` take effect, and each of them "Requires multilayer". Community Shaders' `Lighting.hlsl` (MULTI_LAYER_PARALLAX path, ~2626-2640) scales the inner layer's UV by `inner_uv_scale` and blends it by `1 - baseColor.w`, so it shows only where the diffuse alpha is below 1. Coat colour is `PBRParams2`, multiplied by the `_s` coat diffuse only when that map is present. So neither needs coat diffuse.
- **AJ3. Hover text.** Coat colour: "Flat coat tint; multiplied by the _s coat diffuse map when Coat diffuse is on". Inner UV scale: "UV scale of the inner layer (multilayer parallax). Visible only where the diffuse alpha is below 1 and an inner-layer texture is set".
- **AJ4. Multilayer off keeps the values.** The group is hidden; the stored coat values stay in `sdc-project.toml` and the writer skips them (its existing rule: a group key is written only while its feature is live). A note under the checkbox reads "N coat values kept, unused while Multilayer is off".
- **AJ5. Every colour uses the colour field.** The colour rows (`emissive_color`, `subsurface_color`, `coat_color`, `fuzz.color`) all draw through `pgp_fields::control`, which uses `ui::color::color_field` (swatch, colour square, hex) with the R/G/B boxes beside it. The screenshot that showed R/G/B only predates that control; there was no second drawing path. The JSON editor lists keys from `KEYS` and does not use `advanced`, so it is unchanged.
- **AJ6. Skipped: the "Inner UV scale has no visible effect without an inner-layer texture" note.** The set model has no inner-layer slot (`SlotKind::Coat` is the `_cnr` in slot 7), so the pitfall cannot be decided.
- **AJ7. Member textures switch features on (owner).** A PBR set's feature keys turn on from its members: `_cnr` turns on `multilayer` and `coat_normal`; `_f` turns on `fuzz`; `_p` turns on `parallax`; `_g` (the emissive member) turns on `emissive`; `_s` turns on `coat_diffuse` when multilayer is on, else `subsurface` (the schema: `subsurface` and `coat_diffuse` both put `_s` in slot 8). Glint has no texture and is not touched. See `model/auto_flags.rs`; `pbr_follow.rs` only follows sub-types, so nothing was extended there.
- **AJ8. They are defaults, not writes.** An auto value shows "(auto)" with the reason on hover ("on because the set has a _cnr"). An explicit value, including off, wins: unticking an auto-on switch stores `false`. Removing the member removes the auto value. Nothing is stored in `sdc-project.toml` unless the user sets it.
- **AJ9. A feature an explicit one rules out stays off.** Keys are resolved in order against the exclusion table, so an explicit `hair` keeps multilayer, fuzz and the rest from turning on.
- **AJ10. The writer uses the resolved value** (`json_entries`): a set with a `_cnr` writes `multilayer: true` and `coat_normal: true` with no explicit value. Export pitfalls still read the explicit settings only.
- **AJ11. Fuzz and coat normal keep their own reason.** A filled coat slot greys Fuzz with "the set has a coat normal", and a filled fuzz slot greys the coat normal switch; the member reason is now checked before "off: multilayer is on", because a coat normal turns multilayer on by itself.

## Note AK — Hair diffuse is cutout + blend (owner, 2026-10-14)

- **AK1 Hair member default (amends S-shader-5).** A `hair` diffuse now defaults to `alpha.policy = cutout_blend`, with `mips.coverage = on` (was `cutout`). Owner: "Hair usually has two shapes, a no-suffix hair nif with alpha blending and testing, then another with just testing." `cutout_blend` keeps coverage-preserving mips for the tested shape and keeps the soft edge for the blended one. Mesh evidence still beats the shader default (Note U7). The engine version stays 0.7.0. Hair diffuse outputs change bytes once, so `if-changed` re-encodes them.

## Note AL — ORM packing input (2026-10-15)

- **AL1 `_orm` is a packing input (owner).** An `_orm` file (raw-map role `orm`, exporter vocabulary term `orm`, a packing input like `_roughness`) supplies three slots from its channels: AO ← R, roughness ← G, metallic ← B. It starts the cs `_rmaos`/`_rfaos` recipe (so its set is PBR) and the cpm `_m`, and feeds the vanilla env-mask `_m` as a metallic source. The cpm `_m` G is 1 − its G; its R env mask and B both take its B. The channel is read directly, whatever `channels.grayscale_source` says, and the file is never flagged `CHAN-NOT-GRAY`. Slots it does not hold (specular, height, fuzz) come from their own maps, `packing.fill.*` or the default exactly as before, so an ORM-only `_rmaos` alpha is `packing.fill.specular`, else 1. A plain `channels.swizzle` cannot give that, which is why ORM is an input and not a swizzle.
- **AL2 Loose maps win, with an info note.** A loose map for a slot the `_orm` also holds wins that slot (glossiness counts as a roughness source, roughness as a glossiness source). Each such slot raises `PACK-ORM-OVERRIDDEN` (info) on the packed job, naming both files; it is not a conflict. An `_orm` that every slot of the recipe takes from loose maps is `PACK-UNUSED-INPUT` ("a loose map is used for each channel it could supply"). A second `_orm` in one set is unused, as for any role (Note M13).
- **AL3 Representation.** `ChannelSource::Source` gains an optional `channel` (0..=2), serialised only when set, so a job without an `_orm` keeps its JSON and manifest bytes; provenance reads `iron_orm.png:G`. The packed source's role is `orm`. `RawMap::ALL` stays the eight single-channel roles that have a `packing.fill` (`--fill orm=…` is refused); `RawMap::INPUTS` adds `orm` where a file name is mapped to a role. The engine version stays 0.7.0; outputs of sets without an `_orm` are unchanged.
- **AL4 RNM bent AO reads an `_orm` (fix round 1).** The bent layer's AO source (Note W9) is a loose `_ao` (R), else an `_orm` (R), else an `_rmaos`/`_rfaos` file (B). A set with an `_orm` and no `_ao` no longer raises `RNM-AO-MISSING`.
- **AL5 `_rfaos` notes the dropped ORM metallic (fix round 1).** A cs set with an `_orm` and a fuzz source (or `packing.recipe = rfaos`) builds `_rfaos`, which has no metallic channel. The `_orm` is still used for R and B, so it is not `PACK-UNUSED-INPUT`; the packed job gets a `PACK-ORM-OVERRIDDEN` info note naming the unused B instead. That code now covers any `_orm` channel left unused.
- **AL6 An `orm` alias names this note (fix round 1).** A user preset with `suffix_aliases.orm` is still refused (`CFG-INVALID`, the vocabulary rule of spec §3.7); the message says "orm is a built-in packing input since Note AL" so a preset written before this change is easy to fix.

## Note AM — MO2 button and preset creator (2026-10-15)

- **AM1 One MO2… button (owner design §1).** The scan source label, *Choose MO2 instance…*, *Scan a folder…* and *Scan* left the scan rows. Both headers (Textures and Meshes) hold one **MO2…** button whose hover text is the source ("MO2 instance X, profile Y", "Folder X", "No source chosen"). It opens a `path_picker::menu` popup (AG125's `CloseOnClickOutside`): the instance path with **Choose…**, the instance's profiles, **Scan a folder…**, and **Scan** or **Cancel**. The profiles are a flat list of selectable labels, not a combo, so no nested popup can close the menu. A profile is any folder under the instance's profiles folder that holds a `modlist.txt`, read in the model when the instance is set (not per frame), sorted case-insensitively; picking one is `use_instance` (saved in `gui.toml`). Choosing and scanning are off while a scan runs or a picker is open, as before.
- **AM2 The scan rows show progress only.** While a scan runs the row keeps the spinner, the progress bar with its ETA and a Cancel (so a scan can stop without opening the popup). The Textures view draws its scan row only when it has something to show (a scan, a status line or an error); the MO2 question dialog is drawn every frame either way.
- **AM3 Preset creator: the draft is the preset's TOML table.** `model::presets::PresetsModel` edits the preset's own table, so what the creator does not show (`defaults`, a description, other rules) is kept. A field edit writes `[subtypes.<id>].<field>`; ↺ removes that key and the tables it leaves empty, so the base shows through. The shown values are the engine's layering (`registry.chain(base)` plus the draft, `layers_of`, `merge_json`, `finish_profile`), with the resolve's backend follow (`backend_follows_format`) applied for display; the hint is `registry.profile(base, target, st)`. The target is the preset's own, else its chain's, else the GUI's.
- **AM4 Validation through the engine loader.** A draft is checked by loading its TOML into a copy of the registry (built-ins plus the presets folder via `add_dir`, as a plan loads it), with the edited preset removed first: `add_toml` (aliases, reserved and duplicate ids, the schema), then `check_preset` (every sub-type under every allowed target, every rule). Save is offered only when that passes, and `save` runs it again before writing. Engine change: `PresetRegistry::remove` is now `pub` (was `pub(crate)`) for this; no behaviour changed.
- **AM5 Rows and subheaders.** Groups: Colour (category colour), Normal (category normal), Data (grayscale and mixed/packed), Special (HDR and `complex_grass`), each in `Subtype::ALL` order. A row's title is the sub-type and its resolved format. Compression offers `fields::encoding_options` (the `allowed_encodings` pairs, format and tag together), the encoder (only backends that fit the format: Built-in, and NVTT for BC7 and BC6H) and effort; a format pick drops the preset's own backend for that sub-type when it cannot write the new format. Resize & mips: resize, levels, filter (only filters `filter_option_lock` accepts) and coverage, shown for the cutout policies only. Alpha (policy, and threshold for cutout) shows where the base's format keeps an alpha channel. Locks are `fields::field_lock`. Category, content and the sub-type's own rules are never offered.
- **AM6 Preset names.** The name is the id and the file name: lower-case letters, digits and '-', not starting with '-'. It is checked live, before the engine's own id rules (`sdc-base` reserved, no id loaded twice).
- **AM7 Duplicate and New.** Duplicate copies the shown table under `<id>-copy` (then `-copy2`, …) without its `suffix_aliases`: the copy and the original could not both load with them (Note T8). New starts on the GUI target's quality preset, with that target.
- **AM8 Save, rescan, Delete.** A user preset is written back to the file it was loaded from (its file name need not match its id); a new one goes to `<folder>/<id>.toml`, refused when that file exists (a preset that did not load stays untouched). The folder is `AppModel::user_presets` only: `%APPDATA%\sdc\presets` in the app, and in tests `None` (Save then refuses) or a temporary folder. After a save or delete the folder is scanned again and the project re-planned, so the preset applies without a restart. Delete (user presets only) asks first, removes the file and rescans. Reset preset drops the whole `[subtypes]` table; the name, aliases, remaps, `defaults` and rules stay.
- **AM9 Suffixes and aliases.** A row's built-in suffixes are the §6.1 suffixes `suggest::suggest` maps to the sub-type in any context that changes its answer (a PBR, `_msn` or Advanced skin set; varying alpha; a landscape or interface path). The aliases listed are the draft's aliases whose canonical suffix is one of them. A new alias is checked by loading a probe preset that holds only that alias into the registry copy, so the message is `check_aliases`' own (without its preset prefix); Add is enabled only when it passes. A sub-type that no §6.1 suffix names (`skin_wet`, `hdr_sky`, …) takes no alias.
- **AM10 Channel remaps and fills.** Data rows only. A swizzle remap is a suffix alias plus a rule `match = { glob = "*_<alias>.*" }`, `overrides = { channels = { swizzle = "<4 of r,g,b,a,0,1>" } }`. It is a glob because a rule's `match.suffix` sees the canonical suffix (the alias is already mapped), and a glob without '/' matches the file name. A rule of exactly that shape is shown as its alias's remap; removing the alias removes the rule. The `rmaos` row shows the ORM → RMAOS example (Note AL) as text only: it is engine behaviour and is never written to a preset. Packing fills (roughness, metallic, ao, specular, height, 0-255) are `[subtypes.<id>.packing.fill]` keys, with the base's value, or "neutral default", as the hint.
- **AM11 The window.** "Presets…" in the Textures header and the palette action `presets` open it; it also draws in the Meshes view. It is a resizable `egui::Window`, and its rectangle is kept in `gui.toml` as `[layout] presets_window`, like the 3D settings window (AG132). A built-in shows read-only, with Duplicate. The creator's button is "Save preset", since the header has its own Save. A collapsible TOML preview shows exactly the text Save writes.
- **AM12 The header's Preset menu (owner design §2 "appears in the Mode/preset picker").** The GUI had no preset picker (gui.md said so). A **Preset** combo after Mode lists "default (<the Mode's quality preset>)" and every selectable preset, built-in or from the presets folder, whose chain's target is the Mode's or unset; it reads the creator's registry, so a saved preset is listed at once. A pick writes `preset = "<id>"` to `sdc.toml` (default removes it) as one undo step and re-plans. A preset of another target is not offered; one already in `sdc.toml` still shows as the selection.
- **AM13 Review fixes (Opus review, 2026-10-15).** Amends AM7, AM8 and AM10.
  - Duplicate also drops the remap rules (`*_<alias>.*`, AM10) of the aliases it drops, so the copy holds no orphan rule.
  - Save edits a user preset's own file in place with `toml_edit`. Keys whose value did not change keep their text, comments and order; a changed table is synced key by key, and anything else that changed is replaced. A new preset is written as `toml` serialises it. The TOML preview shows that same text, and the engine loader validates it. The write is atomic: a `<file>.toml.tmp` in the same folder, renamed over the file.
  - The Delete prompt names the file it removes, which may differ from the preset's id.
  - The ORM example notes that a loose `_specular` map wins over `packing.fill.specular`.
- **AM14 A deleted preset named in `sdc.toml`.** Before this fix, a plan whose `sdc.toml` named a preset that was gone failed outright: an error dialog ("Planning failed: unknown preset 'x'") and no plan. Now the GUI leaves that `preset` out of the plan's inline config (the plan uses the user config's preset, else the Mode's default) and raises the banner "preset <id> not found: using <fallback>". `sdc.toml` keeps the name, so a restored file applies again; picking a preset in the header clears the banner. The check reads the presets folder only for an id that is not built in. The CLI is unchanged: there an unknown preset is still an error.
- **AM15 Unsaved edits ask first.** Select, New and Duplicate over a draft with unsaved edits (a new or duplicated preset counts until it is saved) open a modal: "Discard unsaved changes to <id>?" with Discard and Cancel. Esc or a click outside cancels (`dialogs::modal_keys`), and Enter does nothing (Discard loses work).
- **AM16 Broken preset files show in the window.** The files of the presets folder that did not load (CFG-PRESET-SKIPPED) are listed at the top of the window: file name, the engine's reason, the full path on hover. An **Open folder** button opens the folder in Explorer.
- **AM17 Startup banners survive a re-plan.** `replan` rebuilds the banner (AM14), which would have wiped a banner `refresh_banner` does not build, such as the startup "Settings were not read, defaults are used". Such a banner is now `AppModel::kept_banner` (`keep_banner`): every rebuild puts it first, beside the built lines, until the user clicks **Dismiss** on the banner row.

## Note AN — PGPatcher match and lock (2026-10-15)

Evidence: PGPatcher source (`PGLib/src/patchers/PatcherMeshShaderTruePBR.cpp`), the wiki's Mod-Authors page, and the owner's live test (2026-10-15, `s6 pgp tests.json`, T1-T5): `match_diffuse` + `rename` renamed; `match1` + `match_diffuse` + `rename` renamed because the diffuse hit came first; `match1` + `rename` patched nothing; `lock_normal` kept the vanilla normal; `lock_normal` + `slot2` let slot2 win.

- **AN1 One match key per entry.** PGPatcher ORs the match keys (match_normal, match_diffuse, path_contains, matchN; the first hit wins) and applies `rename` by cutting the config's `match_diffuse`/`match_normal` from the end of the path, so a hit through a `matchN` gives a mangled path. A renamed set's entry is now `match_diffuse` + `rename` only, with no `matchN`. This replaces AA8's "the match slots stay the entry's matches". `match_diffuse` is still the lowest active match with its slot suffix cut, else the derived diffuse (AA8; AF8's short-match warning still applies). An entry without a rename writes `matchN` only, as before. An old project that stored `match1`/`match2` next to a rename keeps those keys but exports one match key. `path_contains` is not written on a renamed set either (coordinator ruling, 2026-10-15): PGPatcher cuts a `path_contains` hit by `match_diffuse`'s length too, so it gets a mangled path. The set editor's Matching header and the JSON editor say "renamed: matches by diffuse (PGPatcher rename needs match_diffuse)". The plugin lookup (`plugin_refs::set_key`) reads a renamed entry the same way.
- **AN2 No extra match on a renamed set.** An edit that would give a renamed set a second match row is refused in `set_json_value` (`prefix::extra_match_refusal`): a `matchN` path, an empty + row, or `match1` back on next to another match. The card's "Match on this", the Matching `+` menu and the JSON editor's `+ key > Match` entries are disabled with the reason. Adding a `path_contains` to a renamed set is refused the same way. Existing extra rows and an existing `path_contains` stay editable and removable. Each says "not written", and a pitfall (export asks) says so.
- **AN3 Right-click Lock is PGPatcher's slot lock.** The slot menu (card and pill member) gains "Keep original texture (PGPatcher lock)", or "Stop keeping original texture (PGPatcher lock)" when on. It toggles the set's `lock_<slot>` key: on stores `true`, off removes the key (`false` is the default, so it is never stored). It is one `set_json_value` call, so one undo step, and the same key the Texture locks group shows. It is offered only on sets that write a PGPatcher entry (`is_json_set`: a PBR set under CS); elsewhere it is disabled with the reason.
- **AN4 The sdc slot lock is "Pin slot (sdc)".** F3a's lock (`lock = ["slotN"]` in sdc-project.toml, which refuses moves into the slot) is unchanged. Its menu entry is renamed "Pin slot (sdc)" / "Unpin slot (sdc)"; the card and member hovers and the Slot commands dialog ("Pin slot N", "Pinned: …") say pin too.
- **AN5 Slot map; the cubemap is left out.** 1 `lock_diffuse`, 2 `lock_normal`, 3 `lock_emissive`, 4 `lock_parallax`, 6 `lock_rmaos`, 7 `lock_cnr`, 8 `lock_subsurface` (`prefix::lock_key`). `lock_cubemap` clears slot 5 instead of keeping it, and no card has slot 5, so the menu does not offer it. The Texture locks group relabels it "Clear cubemap".
- **AN6 What a lock does.** A locked slot's card shows a big red ✕ over the thumbnail, with no caption (owner, 2026-10-15). The card has one hover, in place of the thumbnail's path: "PGPatcher lock (lock_<slot>): the original texture in this spot is used", then, with a scan, the slot's textures in the meshes that use the set's diffuse. It is read on a worker per (diffuse, slot) like the Used-by list, shows "…" until it lands, and a finished scan clears it so it is read again; a read that panics keeps its error as the answer (review M1). A lock key on a set that writes no JSON shows "lock stored, not written (no PGPatcher entry)" instead of the cross (review M2). Its pill member shows a small red ✕. Export still encodes the file, since other sets may use it. PGPatcher applies `slotN` after the locks, so the writer drops a slot path sdc derives itself (a borrowed fill, or a texture not at the set's own path) for a locked slot. An explicit slot command (`slotN` in the settings) is still written and raises the pitfall "slotN overrides the lock (PGPatcher applies slotN last)". The brief's optional "drop the slotN" offer is not built: the slot command's own From this set does it.

## Note AO — Input name editor (2026-10-15)

Owner: "change it to a text input with dropdown and visible gray autocomplete through tab. Also add a moveable lock slider (binding to each /) that keeps paths from that point backwards."

- **AO1 A text box replaces Input name….** The set header shows the input name in a text box (`ui::input_name::picker`) with a ▾ at its right. Enter, focus loss with a valid changed value, or a pick in the ▾ list commits through `AppModel::set_input_name`: one undo step, the same path as before (Note AA, G1). Esc reverts. Focus loss with an unchanged value (case, `\` and `.dds` aside) or an invalid one reverts. While the text is invalid (`input_name_plan` refuses it: P13 path rules, AA's alias checks), the box has a red outline and the reason as hover text (also the box's accessible description).
- **AO2 Gray completion.** The candidates are the ▾ list's (`known_textures(Some(0))`, sorted). The ghost is the rest of the first path that starts with the text (case-insensitive, `\` read as `/`), drawn gray after the text while the box has focus. None when no path starts with it, or when the first one is the text itself. Tab takes the ghost up to and including its next `/` or `\`, or all of it when only one path is left (`model::input_name::tab_part`); Right arrow with the cursor at the end takes all of it. While a ghost shows, Tab stays in the box (`TextEdit::lock_focus`) and Shift+Tab still moves focus back; with no ghost, Tab moves focus as usual (and the focus loss commits or reverts, AO1). The ghost itself never commits.
- **AO3 The path lock.** Stops are the start of the text and each separator (`separator_stops`). Lock n keeps everything up to the n-th separator (`lock_cut`); 0 keeps nothing, so the whole path can be edited. The locked part is a dimmed label before the box, the box edits only the rest, Tab completes against the whole path, and the ▾ list shows only paths that start with the locked part. The rail is a thin strip along the box's lower edge: a tick per stop and a handle; a click or drag snaps to the nearest stop. Ctrl+Left/Right move it while the box has focus. It defaults to the last separator of the current name (the folder is locked, the file name is free). The lock is held per set in egui memory for the session and never written to the project. The rail is hidden when the text has no separator.
- **AO4 The live list, and ▾ for browsing (owner via coordinator, 2026-10-15: "a text input with dropdown").** While the box has focus and its text has changed, a list of up to 12 known paths shows right under it, in a foreground `egui::Area` that takes no focus (`live_rows`): the paths that start with the text (case-insensitive, `\` read as `/`; the top one is the ghost's, drawn bold as the best match), or, when none does, the paths under the lock that contain every word of the free part. PGPatcher outputs are never in it (AO8). No row is highlighted until Up or Down moves the highlight, so Enter commits the highlighted row, else the typed text. A click on a row commits that row: the row under a pointer press is recorded before the box sees the press, and the focus loss the press causes commits it instead of the typed text. The first Esc closes the list and keeps the text and the focus (the box's event filter holds Esc while the list shows); a second Esc reverts. A commit or a focus loss closes it. **▾** stays as "show all": the 200-row list with its own filter box and "type more to narrow", under the lock (clicking it ends the box's edit, AO1). Click outside, Esc or a pick closes it (AG125).
- **AO5 Width and the set path.** The box is at least 160 pt with its locked part (its free part at least 56 pt); the free part grows with its text and ghost, up to the room left in the row and 480 pt. The set's path and the box share the header row where the width allows: the path takes what the box leaves, and when that is less than its full width it is shortened with … (the full path on hover) and set in the body size, so the row is no taller than the box; below 40 pt the box starts the next row of the wrapped header (AG126). At 1024×768 in 3D the frame is 499 pt of a 679 pt centre (base 487–489); `set_preview_layout` now asserts 72 %.
- **AO6 Test fixture.** popups.rs clicked "outside" at (800, 880), which now lands on a slot card, whose click opens Slot commands; the test now clicks a background spot and asserts it is one.
- **AO7 One card per PGPatcher slot (owner, 2026-10-15).** The set editor shows at most 8 cards, one per PGPatcher slot: 1 diffuse, 2 normal, 3 emissive `_g` or Advanced skin `_sk`, 4 height `_p`, 5 cubemap (no card, as before: set it with a slot command), 6 RMAOS, 7 coat normal `_cnr` or fuzz `_f`, 8 subsurface `_s` (or a `_coat`, "Coat colour + strength", Note U2). `slot_row` merges the kinds of slots 3 and 7 into one cell: it holds the filled kind, else the first in `SlotKind` order, and a second filled kind is the cell's `clash`, shown in red on the card with `slot_conflict`'s text. Both at once was already an export pitfall ("_cnr and _f both use slot 7", `pbr_export`), and a move or drop that would make it is still refused (spec §4.1). Slot 7's card is titled "Coat normal / Fuzz (slot 7)". Its slot commands, its right-click PGPatcher lock (`lock_cnr`) and Pin act on slot 7. A file drop and the slot menu's "link to" entries accept any kind of the card's slot, so an `_f` drops on the slot 7 card. Members that are not slot textures (packing inputs, RNM layers, the complex `_m`) keep their own UI.
- **AO8 No PGPatcher outputs in the input name box (owner, 2026-10-15).** Paths under `textures\pbr\` (case-insensitive) are PGPatcher's outputs, not input names, so `input_name_candidates` leaves them out: they are never ▾ rows or gray completions. Typing one by hand is still checked by the usual rules.
- **AO9 The 3D Mesh list order (owner, 2026-10-15).** The scanned candidates are ordered in four tiers: loose normal, BSA normal, loose ground/first-person, BSA ground/first-person (`order_candidates`, a stable sort after the query, so each tier keeps the query's order). A mesh counts as BSA when its copy's origin is `KIND_BSA` (`Store::copy_location`); a copy gone from the scan counts as loose. A ground or first-person mesh is one whose file stem (case-insensitive) contains `gnd`, ends with `_go` (a whole suffix, so "dragon" and "gone" do not count), or starts with `1stperson` (`is_ground_or_first_person`). Auto draws the first usable candidate, so it now prefers a loose, normal mesh.
- **AO10 Review fixes (Opus review, 2026-10-15).** Amends AO2, AO4, AO7 and AO9.
  - The ghost is drawn after the box's edit, from the text as edited that frame, and an edit re-sets the box's focus filter (Tab held while a ghost shows, Esc while the live list shows) and asks for a repaint, so the next frame's Tab or Esc already follows the new text.
  - A live row commits only when the press and the release both land on it. A press dragged off and released elsewhere is no click: nothing is committed and the box keeps its text and focus.
  - A pasted path that starts with the locked part (case-insensitive, `\` read as `/`) loses that part, so it is not doubled after the lock.
  - The box's `input_name_plan` check is cached per (text, `items_rev`), and the ▾ list's paths under the lock per (path list, locked part).
  - A clashing card (slot 3 `_g` + `_sk`, or slot 7 `_cnr` + `_f`) shows both members: a red line "<file> and <file> both use slot N: remove one", then a row per member with its thumbnail and file name (click selects, double-click expands) and ✕ ("Remove <file>"), which takes it out of the set (`remove_member`). The `_g` + `_sk` export warning ("_g and _sk both use slot 3") already existed beside slot 7's in `pbr_export`.
  - An empty slot 3 card is titled "Emissive / Skin (slot 3)". A filled one keeps its kind's title, and slot 7's card is always "Coat normal / Fuzz (slot 7)". "From another set" names both kinds of the slot when no other set has one ("no other set has a Coat _cnr or Fuzz _f").
  - A ground model is a stem with a whole `gnd` token (split on `_`, `-`, space and digits), or a stem ending in `gnd` or `_go`. "signdoor" and "designdetail" do not count.

## Note AP — Preset suffix branches (2026-10-15)

Owner: "suffixes should lead small branches for presets when they are present; no suffix = default behaviour". No engine change, except AP10 (rules layer).

- **AP1 The sub-type's own settings are the "no suffix" default.** `[subtypes.<id>]` is unchanged. Under Suffixes, every suffix that names the sub-type (AM9's built-in suffixes, then the draft's aliases whose canonical suffix is one of them) is its own collapsed branch, indented, titled `_<suffix> — <format> (overrides N)` (an alias: `_<alias> → _<canonical> — …`). N counts the fields the branch sets, a format and its tag as one. Inside is the row's tree: Compression, Resize & mips, Alpha (where the row shows it), Channel remap and Packing fills (data rows). Each field hints the sub-type's resolved value ("sub-type: …") and has ↺. `PresetsModel::{branch_field, set_branch_field, clear_branch_field}`; `Row::branches` holds each branch's profile (the row's layered document plus the rule's overrides, `finish_profile`, the backend follow of AM3).
- **AP2 A branch is one rule, after the other rules (amended by AP10).** A branch with overrides is a rule `match = { suffix = "<canonical>", subtypes = ["<id>"] }` (built-in) or `match = { glob = "*_<alias>.*", subtypes = ["<id>"] }` (alias, since `match.suffix` sees the canonical suffix), with the fields in `overrides`. An emptied branch's rule is removed, and an empty `rules` with it. A source's matching rules layer in file order (AP10), so a new built-in suffix branch is inserted after the hand-written rules and before the alias branches, and a new alias branch goes last. A branch therefore changes only the fields it sets: a duplicated cs-compact's opaque-colour rule (bc1, resize none) still gives `_d` files BC1 under a `_d` resize branch, and an alias branch adds to its canonical suffix's branch (its files carry the canonical suffix too). Rules of the base presets are an earlier source and apply underneath. Rules sit at file reach before `[files]` and `--set`, so those still win (end-to-end tests). A file saved before AP10 with its branches first is read the same way; only new branches are placed by this order.
- **AP3 Branch rules are recognised by shape.** `Rule` has no name field (`deny_unknown_fields`), so the editor owns a rule exactly when it has only `match` and `overrides`, and `match` has only `subtypes` (one sub-type) plus either `suffix` (one of that sub-type's built-in suffixes under the draft's target) or `glob` equal to `*_<alias>.*` for one of its aliases. A hand-written rule of that same shape is adopted as a branch; it means the same thing. The glob stays AM10's `*_<alias>.*`, not the brief's `**/*_<alias>.*`: a glob without '/' also matches the file name alone, so both match, and old remap rules keep one form.
- **AP4 Other rules are read-only and kept.** Every rule that is not a branch (base-style multi-sub-type rules, AM10 remap rules without `subtypes`, anything else) is listed under "Other rules (N, read-only)" above the groups, as TOML, and kept in its order after the branches.
- **AP5 Reset preset** drops `[subtypes]` and every branch rule; names, aliases and other rules stay (amends AM8).
- **AP6 Remaps live in the branch.** AM10's separate remap list and "New remap" row are gone (`add_remap`, `remaps` removed). Channel remap is a section of every data tree: four source pickers writing `channels.swizzle` to the scope (the sub-type table or the branch rule). A remap for an alias is that alias's branch's Channel remap. Removing an alias removes every rule matched only by its glob (its branches and an AM10 remap rule); Duplicate does the same for the aliases it drops (AM13). The ORM → RMAOS example is a read-only `_orm → _rmaos` branch header under rmaos holding the example text; nothing is written.
- **AP7 Add alias opens its branch.** A successful Add alias opens the new alias's (empty) branch on the next frame. The New alias box is labelled for accessibility.
- **AP8 Built-in suffixes are cached per target.** `suffixes_of` makes thousands of `suggest` calls, and the branch list and classifier need it on every refresh, so `PresetsModel::suffixes(st)` caches it for the draft's target.
- **AP9 Families: PBR / CS, then CPM, then Vanilla (owner, 2026-10-15: "glow next to PBR emissive is confusing").** Amends AM5. The body has three collapsing family headers, open by default, each with a hint ("PBR / Community Shaders — used under the cs target"; "Complex Parallax Materials (cpm) — used under the cs and cpm targets"; "Vanilla — used under every target"). Inside a family the AM5 groups (Colour, Normal, Data, Special) show where they have rows. The family is derived from `policy::availability`, not listed: available under cs only → PBR / CS (pbr_*, coat_normal, rmaos, skin_*, flow, hdr_sky); under cs and cpm but not vanilla → CPM (complex_material); anything vanilla also has → Vanilla. Each sub-type is in exactly one family (`presets::layout`, tested). `availability`, not `offered`, decides, since `offered` hides complex_grass under every target. complex_grass, which the engine makes available under all three targets, is placed in PBR / Community Shaders (Special) by hand (owner, 2026-10-15): it is a Community Shaders feature in practice.
- **AP10 Every matching rule of a source layers (owner, 2026-10-15; engine change).** Before, `policy::first_match` applied only the first matching rule of each source. Now `policy::matching` yields every matching rule in file order and each is applied in turn, so a later rule overrides only the fields it sets and keeps the earlier rules' other fields; each field's provenance names the rule that last set it (`rule:<source>#<i>`). The precedence between sources (preset chain base first, user config, project config) and between layers (rules at file reach, before analysis facts, suffix facts, `[files]` and `--set`) is unchanged. The mesh-alpha check sees an explicit `alpha.policy` if any matching rule sets one. The sub-type decision is unchanged: the first matching rule of a source decides, and a source whose first match names no `subtype` leaves the decision to the next (pinned by `cli_convert::accepted_suggestion_loses_to_rules_which_lose_to_set`; the ruling is about field overrides). While a sub-type layers, a rule naming another `subtype` still does not match (T10 #3). This applies to config rules as well as preset rules. Audit of the built-in presets before the change: `sdc-base`'s two rules (`**/interface/**` and `**/sky/**`, both `mips.levels = none`) both match only a path with both components, and give the same value (only the provenance index moves, to `sdc-base#1`); `cs-compact`'s and `vanilla-compact`'s two rules match `alpha = none` and `alpha = constant255`, which never hold together. No built-in output changes; the goldens are unchanged. Engine version stays 0.7.0.
- **AP11 A branch shows what its files get (Opus review, 2026-10-15).** A branch's header format and profile are the row's layered document, then every rule a plan would apply to a file with that suffix, in order: the chain's rules (base first), then the draft's, so a hand-written rule, the canonical suffix's branch under an alias's, and the branch itself. Whether a rule applies is decided from what the branch knows (the sub-type, the canonical suffix, the alias's glob); a rule on another path glob or on `set_has` cannot be known and is left out. A rule on alpha content is not guessed: it is left out and the header says "(+ rules by alpha)". A branch field's hint shows the same profile without the branch's own rule ("inherits: …"), so `_d` = bc1 under `_foo` = resize half shows `_foo` as BC1 at half size. A new built-in branch goes after the last hand-written rule and before the first alias branch after it (amends AP2).
- **AP12 complex_material stays in CPM (merge of nif/main, 2026-10-15).** nif/main's AT1 makes `complex_material` available under vanilla, so the AP9 derivation would put it under Vanilla and leave the CPM family empty. It is placed in CPM by hand, as complex_grass is in PBR / CS, and the CPM hint now reads "complex parallax materials (used under every target since vanilla keeps a complex _m)". Owner to confirm.

## Note AQ — 2D preview gamma (2026-10-15)
- **AQ1 Cause.** The expanded 2D preview drew an sRGB colour map's Source pane darker and more contrasty than its Encoded pane. `SourcePreview.levels` was `prep.chain` (`engine/src/session.rs`, `preview_source` and `preview`): the chain in the engine's working domain. For sRGB colour that is linear light (`ops::preprocess_with` step 5 decodes sRGB before the mips); for normals, MSN and flow it is −1..1 vectors. The encoded side is the stored bytes (`quantize::to_encoder_input` re-encodes; the textures are UNORM and the CPU decode gives stored 0..1). The shader and every GUI reader (AG55 readout, heatmap, Split, lens, tiling) treat both sides as stored values, so the source was shown as linear values read as sRGB: grey 128 drew as about 55. The shader, the texture formats, the sRGB / UNORM toggle and the tone map were right.
- **AQ2 Fix.** The engine maps the source side to stored values once, with the encoder input's own per-domain rule (`quantize::stored_rgb`, which `to_encoder_input` now calls too): `Session::preview` and the new `Session::preview_source_stored` (used by the GUI worker) return stored values; `preview_source` keeps the working chain, which the golden tests read. `Prepared` carries the work's domain (complex grass builds a `Stored` work, so the profile alone cannot tell). The values are not rounded to 8 bits. HDR and stored-value domains are unchanged, so the tone map and exposure path is untouched; alpha is untouched.
- **AQ3 Readers fixed with it.** Source pane, heatmap against the source, pixel readout source line (its "stored values, sRGB-encoded" label is now true for both sides), the normal readout's source vector (AG56 unpacks 0..1, which the source now is), Split, lens and tiling (same slots). Pin and compare use decoded images only and were right. The UNORM toggle still re-encodes the raw stored values with `linear_to_srgb` on both panes alike (AG55).
- **AQ4 Set preview (review follow-up).** The set preview's Source mode (`model/set_preview.rs`) had its own copy of the mapping (`stored_levels`, with the domain from `domain_for(profile)`). It now reads `preview_source_stored` and keeps only what the GPU samples per format (one or two channels, no alpha for BC6H). For complex grass `domain_for` and the work agree today (both `Stored`), so nothing visible changes; the copy is gone so the two cannot drift. An engine test checks the stored source equals the encoder input for MSN, flow and complex grass.

## Note AR — Crash 2026-10-15 (staging buffer)

Evidence: `crash-20261015-132201.log`, a panic at `egui-wgpu-0.36.2/src/renderer.rs:984`: "Failed to create staging buffer for index data. Index count: 8556. Required index buffer size: 34224. Actual size 209160 and capacity: 209160". It came after 68 minutes, with the JSON editor open, while the owner alt-tabbed to take screenshots. The System log has no 4101 TDR event between 06:50 and 07:40, and no WER entry at 07:30. RADAR_PRE_LEAK_64 fired at 07:11.

- **AR1 Cause: the wgpu device was already lost.** egui-wgpu panics when `queue.write_buffer_with` returns `None`. The size is nonzero and the buffer is big enough, so the `None` came from wgpu 30. There, a validation, out-of-memory or internal error with no error scope and no uncaptured handler goes to `default_error_handler`, which panics with its own "wgpu error: …" message (`backend/wgpu_core.rs` 285-306 and 692). Neither eframe, egui-wgpu nor sdc installs a handler. The one error that returns `None` quietly is `ErrorType::DeviceLost` (line 304), from `check_is_valid()` (`wgpu-core device/queue.rs` 697/751). So the device was invalid before this frame's first write. `device_lost.rs` reproduces the exact message by destroying a device and running `Renderer::update_buffers`.
- **AR2 What lost it is not proven.** The device goes invalid in `Device::lose`, which runs when a hal call returns Lost or Unexpected. That covers queue submit, present and acquire (swallowed through `handle_error_nolabel`), plus `lose_if_oom`, which is off because `MemoryBudgetThresholds::for_device_loss` defaults to `None`. On the headless setup the app uses, the adapter is the RTX 4090 on Vulkan. Vulkan hal maps `VK_ERROR_DEVICE_LOST` to Lost and any unrecognised `VkResult` to Unexpected, and logs neither. A driver or capture-overlay fault during alt-tab and screenshots fits, but nothing recorded it. The app installs no `log` sink, so wgpu's own messages were dropped too.
- **AR3 Not the immediate viewport, not a minimised window.** `update_buffers` runs before `get_current_texture`. A zero-size, minimised or occluded surface takes the `on_surface_status` / SkipFrame path, so it never returns `None` from the queue. The JSON editor is in the stack only because its immediate viewport is the first paint inside the root's `run_ui`; with the editor closed, the root's paint would have panicked the same way. The editor stays an immediate viewport.
- **AR4 Not an egui texture leak.** `egui_textures_do_not_grow_over_frames` cycles items, the JSON editor and the expanded preview for six rounds, and the allocated texture count stays the same as after one. The preview's wgpu textures are fixed slots that are replaced, and the 3D frame texture is `set` in place. A leak would also not explain this panic: a direct out-of-memory error panics with "wgpu error". The RADAR_PRE_LEAK at 07:11 is about a minute before the second render3d GPU initialisation (3010 s into the run, about 07:12). It stays a separate lead for a memory-trace run.
- **AR5 What the app now does.** `crash_log::watch_device` sets wgpu's device-lost callback. The callback writes "wgpu device lost (<reason>, <backend>): <message>" to the run's crash log and a `device_lost` event to the timing log, so the next crash names the reason and the backend. wgpu 30 calls the callback only for Unknown (a driver loss) and Destroyed, never when the device is dropped (`impl Drop for Device`), so a clean exit writes nothing. egui-wgpu cannot recover a lost device and eframe cannot recreate one, so the panic itself still happens.
- **AR6 Unsaved edits survive a crash.** Before this change, the panic hook only logged, and unsaved `sdc.toml` / `sdc-project.toml` edits were lost. At the end of each frame, `SdcApp::ui` stores the dirty documents' text (`AppModel::recovery_snapshot`). The panic hook writes each one to `<file>.recovery` beside the original, and the original is left alone. The user restores by hand, so there is no restore UI.
- **AR7 Recovery files: main thread only, banner, removed on save** (coordinator review). Only a panic on the main thread writes recovery files. A worker's panic is caught and shown as "internal error in …" while the app keeps running, so it writes none. A successful save of a document deletes its `.recovery`. When a folder is opened, or the banner is rebuilt, a `.recovery` newer than its document, or one whose document is missing, adds the banner line "Unsaved edits from a crash were kept in <file>.recovery: rename it over <file> to restore, or delete it." A `.recovery` older than its document is ignored. `crash_log::install_in(dir)` takes the log folder, so the test can run the real hook without touching `%LOCALAPPDATA%`.

## Note AS — PBR grass material type (plan rulings, 2026-10-14) (Note AL on nif/main; renumbered at the merge into main)

Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-pbr-grass-design.md` (owner-approved 2026-10-14, including edits to `crates/engine` `shader.rs`/`api.rs`/`codes.rs`/`analyse.rs`, `schema/`, `crates/gui/src/pgp/` and `model/sets.rs`).

- **AS1 Shader.** `pbr_grass` ("PBR grass", CS only, second in the list after `pbr`) requires albedo, `_n`, `_rmaos` and reads an optional `_s`, with the PBR sub-types. `_g`, `_p`, `_cnr` and `_f` members get `SHADER-EXTRA-MAP`. No member defaults, no encoding change.
- **AS2 Auto-pick.** Inside the PBR rule: a PBR set with a member listed in `BatchRequest::gras_all_diffuses` picks `pbr_grass` (`auto:pbr_grass`). The list is `Store::gras_all_diffuse_textures`, the GRAS slot-0 query of X4 without X5's TruePBR filter, so both pre-patched PBR grass NIFs and vanilla grass NIFs count. The CLI sends none (as X11). An explicit choice wins. A failed scan read keeps the previous grass evidence (the audit rule of Note Y).
- **AS3 Grass view.** `model::sets::build_views` gives a `pbr_grass` set `pgp::settings::grass_view` of its settings: the keys of `pgp::schema::grass_ignores` (emissive, parallax/displacement, hair, coat/multilayer, glint, fuzz, `uv_scale`, `auto_uv`) are dropped, and `subsurface` is on unless set `false`, when the set has an `_s` (without one, export would block on it); since the merge with main the `_s` default comes from the auto flags instead (AS8). The form, export checks, writer and JSON editor read the view (the editor's "+" menu offers no hidden key); `sdc-project.toml` keeps the hidden keys and they come back when the set is switched to `pbr`. Unticking Subsurface on a grass set stores `false` (AS8: main's selector does this for every set).
- **AS4 Warnings.** `PBR-GRASS-DETECT` (warning, job) on the colour member of a `pbr_grass` set whose bottom-left texel has `|L-1| < 0.03 + 0.01` (`Analysis.corner_err`, any shape): CS's grass shadow pass would treat the albedo as complex grass. Single-file jobs only: a packed albedo gets no note (G6). No Grass Lighting warning (owner). A `PBR-GRASS-PGPATCHER` warning was built and removed: PGPatcher 2.1.0 and later patch grass meshes (owner runs 2.2.0), so the docs say once that PBR grass meshes need 2.1.0 or later. Codes 90 → 91.
- **AS5 Preview.** The object PBR path with an amber "approximated" line (`set_preview::GRASS_APPROX`) that does not claim UV scale applies. A `RunGrass.hlsl` render is later work.
- **AS6 Not built.** Flat-lit, opacity ≥ 1 and hidden-key lints; the SLSF2.30 sphere-normal warning; pgpsim's GRAS skip under 1.2.0; hiding the CS texture-set block for grass sets.
- **AS7 Versions.** Engine version stays 0.7.0 (no preset or output change); scan database schema stays 7 (query only).
- **AS8 Merged with main's auto flags (owner, 2026-10-14; amends AS3).** This note was AG, then AL, on nif/main; main's Notes AG (GUI suggestions) and AL (ORM packing input) kept their letters, so it is AS here. Main's member auto flags (AJ7-AJ11, subsurface Standard/Foliage AG102) run for `pbr_grass` sets too, with one guard: `SsHint::grass` (set by `auto_flags::hint`) lets only the `_s` switch a key on, so a `_g`/`_p`/`_cnr`/`_f` member (`SHADER-EXTRA-MAP`) never puts back `emissive`, `parallax`, `multilayer` + `coat_normal` or `fuzz_on`, in the export (`json_entries`) or the form (`pgp_fields::context`), and a `_cnr` cannot turn the `_s` into `coat_diffuse`. The grass view no longer inserts `subsurface = true`: the `_s` turns on `subsurface`, or `subsurface_foliage` by main's Tree Anim/path rule, shown "(auto)"; Off stores `subsurface = false` (main's `sub_edits`). The Subsurface checkbox's grass-only `keep_off` is gone (main's selector replaces the checkbox). Codes 91 → 92 with main's `ALPHA-PARALLAX-NO-ALPHA`.

## Note AT — PGPatcher complex material JSON and the Vanilla complex `_m` (plan rulings, 2026-10-15) (Note AM on nif/main; renumbered at the merge into main)

Spec `F:\ai\dds\nifparseref\plan\complex-material\2026-10-14-complex-material-design.md` (owner-approved 2026-10-14, including edits to `sets_doc.rs`, `pgp/validate.rs`, engine `policy.rs`/`suggest.rs`, the `vanilla-quality` description, `schema/` and two golden tests).

- **AT1 Vanilla keeps a complex `_m` (D16).** The `(Vanilla, complex_material) → envmask` fallback and the "_m under vanilla" suggestion are gone.
  - Vanilla uses the CS rule: a Note L alpha (minimum below 251) gives `complex_material` (High, BC7 UNORM with alpha, BC3 option, Note AH unchanged), and anything else gives `envmask` (BC4, no alpha).
  - Side effects: a Vanilla set with a complex `_m` auto-picks the Complex Material shader. A varying-alpha `_m` that used to be skipped (`LOSS-ALPHA-DATA`) or written as BC4 is now BC7, twice the size, and its `profile_sha256` changes once, so `if-changed` re-encodes it. The engine version stays 0.7.0.
  - The vanilla shader ignores the alpha without ENB. The owner accepted this.
  - Two golden tests changed (both owner-approved). `vanilla_fallbacks_are_written_with_allow_cfg_unavailable` drops `iron_m`: three fallbacks. `vanilla_g_is_glow_and_m_with_alpha_is_envmask_with_loss` now expects BC7 `complex_material` plus `VAL-M-GRAYSCALE` (its RGB is grey); the test name predates AT. It also dropped its `skip_code(None)` assert, because that helper panics when there is no skip event.
- **AT2 `[sets.cm]`.** Per set: `export` plus the schema keys under their schema names. A key that is present is set, and an absent key is unset.
  - `RESERVED` has 13 entries with `cm`.
  - Keys with the wrong type, including a non-bool `export`, stay in the file, are listed and are not exported.
  - Unticking writes `export = false` and keeps the values.
  - The JSON carries only the keys the user set. PGPatcher resets specular colour, specular strength and environment scale to 1 unless the JSON overrides them. This is intended and is documented in the card note (D11).
- **AT3 File and text.** The file is `<job output>.json` under the export root, so `_em`, renames and packed cpm `_m` follow.
  - The text is `$schema` (the schema `$id`) plus the set, well-typed keys, every number a float, serde_json pretty with a final newline.
  - It is validated against the vendored `complex_material.schema.json` (upstream `0176567`, identical at 2.2.0) by `pgp::validate::validate_cm`.
  - Written after a clean export only, on every target; the JSON is written only when the export had no failures. A missing `.dds`, a path clash or an output outside the export root gets a note.
  - The guard is stricter than the CS PBR and PGPatcher exports, which share `pbr_export.rs` (not changed here): the CM export replaces an existing JSON only when its hash matches `[project] cm_json_sha256`. An existing file with no recorded hash is not adopted, even if identical, and an unreadable existing file is kept with a note. Undo keeps the recorded hash.
  - Untick or orphan: the file is left in place, with a note. It is never deleted.
  - Duplicate set ids in `prepare` and `targets`: the first entry decides.
- **AT4 Card.** It shows when the set has a complex `_m`: resolved `complex_material`, or `envmask` under the Complex Material shader. It also shows when a `[sets.cm]` block exists.
  - It holds the export box, the target paths, the D11 note and six key rows, which start unset. A ticked key starts at 1, (1, 1, 1), `true` or glossiness 80. It is never prefilled from a mesh.
  - `card_warnings` takes the targets already computed for the frame, so nothing is re-parsed per target.
  - Amber lines: bad types; written as an env mask (`alpha.policy = remove`); and, after an export only, the `VAL-M-COMPLEX-MARGIN` smallest alpha. That alpha is rounded: 251–254 means CS env mask but PGPatcher complex, and 255 means PGPatcher likely env mask.
  - Placement: in 2D the right column, after the TruePBR panel and the skin card. In 3D, in the TruePBR settings… window (main's AG132), after the skin card and the CS PBR section (merge into main, 2026-10-15).
- **AT5 Plan rulings.**
  - Engine Vanilla tests live in `complex_m_alpha.rs`, not `policy_checks.rs`, because its helpers take a target.
  - `_em`, rename and packed cpm paths are tested at the `targets` level. The write itself is tested once, on `iron_m`, through the same code. One small `finish` test covers a read-only doc.
  - `targets` builds the exporting-sets lookup from one `entries()` call.
  - Notes say "Tessalith", as the CS export does.

## Note AU — GUI suggestions, NIF side (plan rulings, 2026-10-15) (Note AN on nif/main; renumbered at the merge into main)

Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-15-gui-suggestions-nif-design.md` (owner-approved 2026-10-15, including edits to the converter-team GUI files `model/viewport3d.rs`, `ui/viewport3d.rs`, `settings/layout.rs` and the tests `viewport3d.rs`, `pgp.rs`, `window_layout.rs`, `color_fields.rs`, `set_preview_model.rs`, `open_in_textures.rs`). Plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-15-plan-gui-suggestions-nif.md`, rulings R1-R15 and G1-G10.

- **AU1 SLSF names.** `nif::material::{SLSF1_NAMES, SLSF2_NAMES}` (moved verbatim; bit 8 `Receive_Shadows`, SLSF2 bit 23 `Unused01 (TruePBR)`). `model::meshes` re-exports them.
- **AU2 pgpsim `alphaprop_*`.** The four PGPatcher 2.2.0 keys on top of the 1.2.0 simulator (owner D2; a full 2.2 audit is a later plan). Integers go through `uint_in` (2.2 `unsignedFromJSON`): an out-of-range, negative or fractional number is ignored with a range warning; a non-numeric value (a string or a bool) is ignored silently; neither is ever a Skip. Delete beats create; create starts from nifly's 4844/128.
- **AU3 Missing-texture fill (replaces AG76's swap).** `Renderer::set_missing_texture`: colour registers only, t0 and a cube t4 (owner D3); normal, glow, specular and RMAOS stay neutral, and `Bound` is not changed, so a missing data map no longer tints. RFAOS guesses never fill. Each texture entry carries its own missing provenance (`TexEntry.missing`), so the fill follows a slot across material edits and across an override being set and cleared: a slot that was missing before the override is filled again when the override clears, and a slot that was real is never reported missing.
- **AU4 HDRI yaw and RGB background (amends AG74, AG9).** `Scene::env_yaw` turns the backdrop and its ambient (so the HDRI now turns with the sun, which AG74 first ruled out); the GUI passes `sun_yaw` (owner D4). `Background::Solid` is linear RGB; `[layout.look] bg` (sRGB) replaces `grey`. A legacy `grey` is read only when `bg` is missing and is normalised to `bg` on load; it is never written. The grey slider is gone (the colour picker covers it). The settings `sun_yaw` doc comment and AG74 now say the HDRI turns.
- **AU5 UV checker by slot (amends AG75).** `Renderer::override_slot(0, …)`, kept across loads; Lit no longer reloads. The override coexists with the missing-texture fill (the override wins on screen; clearing it restores the fill, AU3). A swap or A/B flip under the override updates the entry and `Bound` but not the screen (R9); a kind mismatch warns "slot N override…" (G8).
- **AU6 3D index.** Supersedes AI2's block-order numbering (merge into main, 2026-10-15). `nif::graph::index3d` (PGPatcher `shapesWith3DIdx`: pre-order, first visit wins) follows nifly's `GetTree` refs, not only block children: node children, `BSTreeNode` bones, particle systems and their data and modifiers, the Havok `modifierRef`, and `NiMeshPSysData.nodeRef`. Particle systems, `NiMeshParticleSystem` included, take an index. Owner-data check: 45 of 46 logged NIFs reproduce the logged indices (the miss is a facegen copy no longer on disk); 1 of 109,320 loose NIFs differs from a block-children-only walk. `shape.index3d`, modscan schema 8 (one full rescan). The Meshes card reads `#index3d`, or `#– (not in scene tree)`.
- **AU7 Debug views (amends AG75).** Albedo, Normals, Roughness, Metallic, AO by one block injected before CS's single `return psout;` (no vendored shader). The anchors are whole-identifier checks: each declaration the block reads is matched on the complete name (boundary on the name, not after `=`), so a longer identifier cannot satisfy it; spacing between type and variable is literal, so a reformatted CS fails closed. Non-PBR Roughness is `1 − glossiness` (G9; CS also has a real Roughness field, noted for the owner) and AO is vertex AO (stand-ins, named in the hover); non-PBR Metallic and FaceGen/Hair/Eye AO are not offered. Without the anchor the views are not offered (`V3Reply::DebugSupport`).
- **AU8 One branch.** All seven items landed on `nif/m-guisug`, not one branch each (R1).

## Note AV — Audit 2026-10-15 fixes

Audit `.superpowers/sdd/audit3/audit.md` (main ce5ab9b).

### Engine

- **AV1 PNG metadata bound (item 1).** A PNG decodes with `max_alloc` = 64 MiB (`load::PNG_METADATA_BUDGET`); every other format keeps no limit. In image 0.25 / png 0.18 that budget covers only the png crate's line buffer and ancillary chunks (the iCCP inflates under it); a static PNG's decoded pixels are not counted, so a 16384 px RGBA16 PNG still loads (a 72 MiB RGBA16 test guards it). The audit's `16384²×8 + 64 MiB` was not used: it would still let a 1x1 file inflate 2 GiB. Over the bound the png crate drops the profile and decodes on, so a bomb loads as its image with no profile reported (the engine ignores profiles anyway). Both `decode` (kra/ora too) and `image_size` use it.
- **AV2 Thumbnail size (item 2, engine part).** `session::THUMB_MAX` 256 → 128 (as `rnm_preview::THUMB_MAX`): a quarter of the memory, 64 KiB per file, ~640 MiB of thumbnails for a 10k folder instead of ~2.5 GiB. The default 96 pt tile is drawn from ≥ its pixel size at 1x; at 1.5x and above, and for tiles over 128 pt, the thumbnail is upscaled (slightly soft). 192 would keep 128 pt tiles sharp on HiDPI for 2.25× the memory; memory was the audit's point. Nothing else reads the thumbnail (the grid only); the GUI's texture eviction is a separate GUI item.
- **AV3 `[files]` matching once per file (item 5).** `overrides::KeyForms` holds a project-relative path's two match forms (lower-cased; lower-cased without the file's last extension), and `path_key_matches` is now `KeyForms::of(rel).matches(key)`, so there is one definition of the rule. `FileScopes::index` (called once at plan time, only when `[files]` has entries) works out every discovered file's forms; `forms_of` reads them by path and file name and falls back to `project_rel` for anything not indexed (a packed output's virtual name, a synthetic input), so the answer is the same as before everywhere. `FileScopes::rel_of` is gone (no other user). Set edits and `apply_output_paths` look keys up in a `KeyIndex` (form → files in file order; the first hit is the old `position`), and the CFG-FILE-UNMATCHED check looks each key up in a set of all forms. Policy fetches a file's forms at most once per pass over the overrides (a lazy cell in `decide_subtype`, `layered_profile_with`, `reencode_requested` and `rnm_target_requested`; `allowed` once per call), so a packed output's virtual name, which is not indexed, is worked out once per pass, not once per key. Keys are still lower-cased per compare and kept as written (messages print them). Override order is unchanged.
- **AV4 Warm re-plan admission (item 14).** Pass 1 checks the source's stamp against the analysis cache before sizing it: a hit is admitted at 0 bytes and its header is not read (it loads nothing). A cold file costs one extra stat. A file that changes between that check and its Load loads unadmitted (a `shortcut:` comment marks it). Pass 2's packed images still read their first source's header (one per recipe, not per file; out of the audit's scope). The CLI's throwaway cache never hits, so CLI output is unchanged.
- **AV5 Duplicates (item 20).** One `fold` (`group::fold`, `pub(crate)`; `sets` imports it) and one `lock` (`api::lock`, `pub(crate)`; `session` imports it); both pairs were identical. `pack::plan` keeps its local `fold` closure (built on `slash`, not in the audit). `Session::preview_source` stays `pub` because the goldens (`tests/golden.rs`, an integration test) call it; it is `#[doc(hidden)]` with a doc line saying the GUI uses `preview_source_stored`. No behaviour change; the existing tests cover it.
- **AV6 Engine follow-ups (review M1, M3).** `apply_set_edits` and `apply_output_paths` return before building the `KeyIndex` when there are no edits / output paths, so a plan without them does no path work (as before AV3); output unchanged. `BakeJob::estimated_bytes` reads the `_n` header through `load::lift_limits` (now `pub(crate)`), so an iCCP bomb is bounded there too; no test (the result is the same, only the transient inflate differs).

### GUI data

Rulings are numbered by audit item (`.superpowers/sdd/audit3/audit.md`), not AV1, AV2, so the four sub-headings do not collide.

- **Item 4 Preset Save checks the file.** A user preset's draft keeps the `SourceStamp` of its file from when it was picked. Save refuses when the file's stamp differs (changed, or deleted) and the window asks **Overwrite** / **Reload** / **Cancel**, as `sdc.toml` does. Overwrite writes the draft over the file (edited in place from the text read at pick time); Reload rescans and picks the preset again, dropping the draft. A new preset or a duplicate has no file and is not checked (the "already exists" refusal still applies).
- **Item 6 Export drawer.** A pack-source or used-layer skip still drops matching queued rows with one pass, but compares paths with `\` and `/` equal byte by byte (no allocation per row), and only marks the job-key index stale; it is rebuilt once at the next lookup. `Row.path` keeps the engine's spelling, so the drawer text does not change.
- **Item 8 Redo of Duplicate.** Redo creates the copy with `create_new`: if anything is at the copy's path, the redo is refused with an error and the file is left alone. After the copy, the step's recorded hash is the new copy's, so undo deletes it even if the source changed between.
- **Item 9 gui.toml write errors.** A failed write is a Warning toast ("gui.toml: could not save: …"), once per distinct error; a successful write resets it.
- **Item 11 Recovery snapshot.** `ProjectDoc` gets a revision from the counter `SetsDoc` uses (Note Y7). The app refreshes the crash-recovery snapshot only when (sdc.toml rev, dirty, sdc-project.toml rev, dirty, read-only) changes.
- **Item 13 One atomic write.** The GUI copies of temp-then-rename (`gui.toml`, presets, `sdc.toml`, `sdc-project.toml`, the PGPatcher / CS PBR / complex material JSONs) are gone; all use `engine::dds::write_atomic` (already `pub`), which syncs before the rename and removes its temporary file on failure. The temporary file name is now the engine's `.sdc-<pid>-<thread>-<n>.tmp`.
- **Item 15 gui.toml hand edits.** The model keeps the settings as last loaded or written, with the file's stamp then. When the stamp changed (and the file exists and parses), the save reads the file and merges field by field, tables recursed: a field the app changed since its last write keeps the app's value, every other field takes the file's. The baseline is the last write, not the session start. A missing baseline (a test setting `settings_path` by hand) writes as before. The app then holds the merged settings.
- **Item 16 JSON hash record.** A failed `sdc-project.toml` save after a JSON write (PGPatcher, CS PBR, complex material) goes through `save_failed`, so a file changed on disk raises the Overwrite / Reload question instead of a dead-end error. When a dialog is already open, the error goes to the export notes, as before.
- **Item 22 Stat cache.** `open_folder` clears the model's `StatCache`. The drawer clears its own cache too (see the review minors below).
- **Review minors (pre-merge review of the GUI data branch).**
  - A project file save refused because the file has the read-only flag (Windows: permission denied on replace) is `SaveError::ReadOnlyFlag`: "sdc-project.toml is read-only: clear the read-only flag to save" (or `sdc.toml`). The file is unchanged and the engine's temporary file is removed.
  - Item 15's merge treats `recent` per folder: the app's list and order, minus folders removed in the file, plus folders added in the file (appended). Other arrays (window rectangles and the like) merge as whole values.
  - A broken `gui.toml` kept as `gui.toml.bad` raises a Warning toast naming the `.bad` file.
  - Item 4's Reload, when the file was deleted or no longer loads: the draft, its edits and the Overwrite / Reload / Cancel row stay, with "<file> was deleted: kept your edits" (or "no longer loads"). The draft keeps its file path, so Overwrite writes the same file.
  - Item 22: `AppModel::drawer_epoch` is bumped by `open_folder` and by a new export; the drawer clears its own `StatCache` when the epoch changes.

### GUI UI

- **AV-UI3 3D frame texture per panel (audit item 3).** The cached frame texture is keyed by `("viewport3d-frame", panel)` (`ui::viewport3d::frame_cache_id`), so the Meshes panel and the set preview, which both count frame ids from 1, no longer show each other's picture.
- **AV-UI2 GPU thumbnail eviction (audit item 2, GUI part).** `ThumbCache` stamps each texture with the egui pass it was last drawn in and, when an upload takes it past `THUMB_CAP` (1000), frees the ones drawn longest ago. A texture drawn in the current or previous pass is never freed, so the tiles on screen stay (the cap can be exceeded by a very tall window). An evicted tile uploads again from the engine's shared thumbnail when it scrolls back.
- **AV-UI12 RNM preview texture (audit item 12).** The blended-preview texture sits in one slot (`rnm_section::preview_texture`) that holds the `Arc<RnmThumb>` and the key; `Arc::ptr_eq` decides reuse, so a reused address cannot show a stale picture. Moving to another set replaces the slot and frees the old texture, so there is one at most instead of one per key ever shown. The RNM layer and mask textures already go through the shared `ThumbCache`, which item 2's cap now bounds.
- **AV-UI7 Textures column stats cached (audit item 7).** `columns::column_stats` keeps the "not in a set" count, the selection summary and the selected-key set in egui temp data, keyed by (items revision, filter, search, sort, active selection, `multi.selection_rev`). Select-all no longer clones the keys and rebuilds two hash sets per frame. `MultiSelection::selection_rev` (the only model edit: the field, its bump in `put_selection` which every set, toggle, range, clear, Ctrl+A and post-plan prune goes through, and `AppModel::reset_multi` for the three resets) only ever grows, so a swap of a middle key can not leave a stale summary. "Need a decision" and "changed on disk" stay a plain per-frame pass: they allocate nothing and `changed_on_disk` moves without an items revision.
- **AV-UI23 Text-field shortcut guard (audit item 23).** `no_shortcut_fires_from_a_text_field_except_ctrl_k` (tests/keyboard.rs) already walked every action key with a `TextEdit` focused and expected only Ctrl+K to fire, and Esc to leave the field without clearing the selection; Esc closing the palette is covered by `enter_in_the_palette_selects_the_texture`. It now also asserts that at least 20 keys were tried and that exactly one action (the palette) fired, so a shrinking action table or a second leak fails it.

### Scan

- **AV-S1 patch-header write errors (item 10).** A failed write in a `patch-header` batch prints `<file>: write failed: <e>`, sets exit 1 and carries on with the next file; it is no longer Fatal (exit 3). The file is not reported patched.
- **AV-S2 texset garbage (item 17).** `Store::delete_unseen` also deletes `texset` rows (and their `tex_use` rows) that no `shape` row and no staged `pending_shape` row uses, and clears the scan's texset cache so a dropped id is never reused. Orphan `path` rows are kept (bounded by distinct paths ever seen).
- **AV-S3 one BSA handle (item 18).** `Bsa::open` keeps the file open and `read` uses positional reads (`seek_read` on Windows, `read_exact_at` on Unix), so `&Bsa` is shared across threads without a lock and a read no longer reopens the path.
- **AV-S4 modlist names (item 19).** A `modlist.txt` name must be exactly one plain folder component. `..`, several components, drive prefixes and absolute names are listed as missing and never joined to the mods folder.
- **AV-S5 BSA folder names (item 21).** `Bsa::open` keeps each folder name once and stores a `u32` folder index per record; the entry name is still `canon_mesh(folder + "\\" + file)` per entry, so empty-folder and padding-space names stay byte-identical to before (test).

## Note AW — Plain normals default to DirectX green (owner, 2026-10-15)

Reverses Note H's "Plain `_normal`: no default green convention" (spec §4.4 "sdc does not guess") for this case only.

- **AW1 Rule.** A plain `_normal`, `_nrm` or `_norm` (vocabulary role `Normal(None)`) whose `channels.green_convention` is unset after layering is treated as DirectX. It no longer blocks, asks or makes the job `unresolved`, and no flip happens.
  - The resolved profile keeps `green_convention` unset (`None`) with the DirectX behaviour (`flip_green` stays false). Output bytes, manifests, profile hashes and `provenance` are identical to a file whose convention was never asked; no `channels.green_convention` provenance entry is invented. The assumption is recorded only by the Info warning (AW2), so provenance never shows it as a user setting.
- **AW2 `IN-GREEN-UNSTATED` is Info.** Message: "green convention of _{term} is unstated; assumed DirectX (set channels.green_convention=opengl if this is an OpenGL map)". It is still raised once per such file. It is not raised when the convention is stated, when `flip_green` is already on, or for `msn`, an unavailable sub-type, or a non-Normal sub-type.
- **AW3 Explicit settings win unchanged.** `--set`, preset, config, rule, GUI setting and an explicit `flip_green` act as before: `green_convention = "opengl"` on a plain `_normal` sets `flip_green`; `_normalgl` sets OpenGL and flips; `_normaldx` is DirectX. `CFG-GREEN-CONFLICT` is unchanged.
- **AW4 Engine surface.** `Need::GreenConvention` is removed from the event schema (`Need` is now `subtype` or `alpha_meaning`; schema file regenerated). A job is no longer `Unresolved` because of the green convention, so `unresolved_code` only ever returns `SUG-LOW-CONFIDENCE`. The JSON event output changes in two ways: a job can no longer carry `needs: ["green_convention"]`, and `IN-GREEN-UNSTATED` has severity `info` instead of `blocking`. `EVENT_SCHEMA` stays 2: the change only removes one enum value and changes a value, no field is added, renamed or retyped, and a consumer written for the old schema simply never sees the old value.
- **AW5 GUI.** `Decision::GreenConvention` and its two-button question are gone; the amber border and the export "undecided" list no longer count it. The info line shows at the top of the settings panel with one *Treat as OpenGL (flip green)* button that writes `channels.green_convention = "opengl"` for the file (`Answer::Green` is kept for that button).
