When the file's own setting is OpenGL the panel shows "Green: OpenGL (flipped)" with a *Treat as DirectX* button that clears the file's `channels.green_convention` (one undo step; disabled when `sdc.toml` is read-only); an OpenGL that comes from `_normalgl` or a preset has no button.
- **AW6 `_norm` and RNM.** W13 said rnm.py reads `_norm` as OpenGL and sdc does not guess; `_norm` now follows AW1 (DirectX unless stated).
- **AW7 Docs.** Spec §4.4/§5/§8 table row, wiki Texture-Types, Troubleshooting, Project-Config, Getting-Started, GUI-Guide and gui.md describe the Info behaviour. RULINGS 41 and Note H are historical.

## Note AX — Version 0.8.0 for the public alpha (owner, 2026-10-15)

- **AX1 Version.** The workspace version moves 0.7.0 → 0.8.0 for the first public alpha (GitHub release of `tessalith.exe` and `sdc.exe`). `ENGINE_VERSION` follows `CARGO_PKG_VERSION` and is part of `is_up_to_date`, so every `--on-existing if-changed` run re-encodes each recorded output once after upgrading. No preset or pixel output changes; goldens are unchanged. `docs/presets-changelog.md` gets a 0.8.0 entry for the same reason.

## Note AY — Exe icon and version resource (owner, 2026-10-15)

- **AY1 Exe resources.** `tessalith.exe` and `sdc.exe` carry the Tessalith icon (`crates/gui/assets/tessalith.ico`) and a VERSIONINFO block (ProductName Tessalith, version from `CARGO_PKG_VERSION`, GPL-3.0-or-later). Each crate's `build.rs` compiles a generated `.rc` with the Windows SDK `rc.exe` (env `RC`, else the newest `Windows Kits\10\bin\<ver>\x64`); no new crate. A missing or failing `rc.exe` only warns and the exe builds without resources.
- **AY2 Window icon.** The GUI sets the window/taskbar icon from the embedded `tessalith-256.png`; a decode failure is logged and skipped.

## Note AZ — cpm target removed (owner, 2026-10-15)

Owner rulings: the targets are `cs` and `vanilla`. Vanilla gains complex grass and complex material. Under vanilla a set packs the complex `_m` (the old cpm recipe) only when its shader is complex material; every other vanilla set keeps the env-mask `_m`, so existing vanilla output is unchanged. `cpm` anywhere is a hard error (unreleased, no alias). `presets/cpm-quality.toml` is deleted. Reverses ruling R1 of the complex-grass design for vanilla.

Supersedes, where they name `cpm`: M1 (the packed `cpm` `_m` is now the vanilla complex-material `_m`; channels and the height default 0 unchanged), M2 (the "AO and fuzz under `cpm`" unused inputs now apply to a vanilla complex-material set), M9 (`m` under `vanilla` only), AL1 (the `_orm` feeds the vanilla complex `_m` where it fed the cpm one), P4 and P22 (the borrowed-slot copies are vanilla only), P24 (see M1), R14 (`cpm-quality` no longer exists), AP9 and AP12 (two preset families, AZ6), S-shader-8 (vanilla only), X3 and the complex-grass design's R1 (vanilla has complex grass, AZ5).

- **AZ1 Hard error.** `Target` is `cs` or `vanilla`; `TargetArg` lost `Cpm`. Serde and clap reject `cpm` in `--target` (convert, validate, patch-header), `sdc.toml`, the user config and a preset's `target`. `--preset cpm-quality` and `base = "cpm-quality"` are unknown presets. No alias was added. The CLI exits 2 for each (tested). `sdc-project.toml` has no target field, so it has nothing to reject. A manifest is read leniently (its `target` is not deserialised as `Target`): an old cpm record no longer matches and re-encodes once under `if-changed`. A user preset in `%APPDATA%\sdc\presets\` with `target = "cpm"` is skipped with `CFG-PRESET-SKIPPED` (Note T6, unchanged; the run goes on), and the warning's reason names the unknown variant `cpm`; selecting that preset is then an unknown-preset error (review M3, tested).
- **AZ2 Trigger.** `pack::recipes_for_with(target, raw, pack_recipe, complex)` and `pack::predicted_suffixes(target, complex, roles)` take whether the set's shader is complex material. `plan_packing` passes `ctx.shaders[set].shader == ComplexMaterial`, so an explicit pick (GUI map, `--set set:<id>.shader`) or an auto pick both count. `cs` ignores the flag. An auto pick needs a member already typed `complex_material` (`ComplexM` rule) and no raw map that starts an `_rmaos` (roughness, glossiness, metallic, AO, `_orm`): those make the set a PBR set, whose `pbr` pick falls back to `default` under vanilla, so `complex` stays false. A raw-map-only set therefore needs the explicit shader. Under an auto pick the raw maps that can remain are height, specular, fuzz and opacity, and these vanilla cases change against main (all sanctioned by "explicit or auto"; tested by `packing::an_auto_picked_complex_material_set_packs_the_complex_m`):
  - **Complex `_m` member + height (with or without specular).** Before: height-only planned no recipe (height `PACK-UNUSED-INPUT`); with specular it planned the env mask. Now the complex `_m` is planned from the height (specular as the env mask). With the default `packing.prefer = prepacked` the existing `_m` is still what is written (bytes unchanged), but the set now gets `PACK-CONFLICT` on the `_m` where height-only had none, and the raw maps' reasons change. With `packing.prefer = raw` the packed complex `_m` replaces the file (before: the file was kept for height only, or replaced by a BC4 env mask with specular).
  - **Complex `_m` member + specular only.** Before: the env mask was planned (`PACK-CONFLICT`; with `prefer = raw` a BC4 env mask replaced the complex `_m`). Now no recipe: specular cannot start the complex `_m`, so it is `PACK-UNUSED-INPUT` ("nothing to pack: the complex _m needs …"), there is no `PACK-CONFLICT`, and with `prefer = raw` the original complex `_m` is kept.
  - Everything else (no complex_material member, or any `_rmaos`-starting raw map) is unchanged.
- **AZ3 Recipe id kept.** The recipe id stays `cpm_m` in events, manifests and the event schema; the Rust variant stays `RecipeKind::CpmM`, because `RecipeKind` serialises its variant name and a rename would change the JSON. Only its doc comment changed (it shows as the schema description).
- **AZ4 Prediction order.** The packing-suffix prediction (Note M7) ran before the shader picks, so an explicit complex-material set with only glossiness or height raw maps would not have predicted its `_m` (a `set_has = "m"` rule would miss it). `explicit_shaders` is now pure and runs before the prediction; its `SET-EDIT-STALE` warnings are still emitted at step 6a, in the same order. Tested by `pack_plan::set_has_sees_the_complex_m_of_an_explicit_complex_material_set`, which fails without the change.
- **AZ5 Complex grass under vanilla.** `Shader::available(Vanilla)` is `ALL[3..]` (the old cpm list, 11 shaders); the `VANILLA` list is gone. `suggest_grass` auto-tags under vanilla. `GRASS-VANILLA` is removed (92 codes; event and validation schemas regenerated). `policy::offered` still hides `complex_grass` from the sub-type pickers on both targets (X3, unchanged).
- **AZ6 GUI.** The Mode menu lists cs and vanilla; the vanilla hover text names complex material and complex grass. The preset creator has two families, PBR / Community Shaders then Vanilla; `family_of` is "cs only → PBR / CS, else Vanilla", so `complex_material` lands in Vanilla by derivation and AP12's hand placement is gone. `complex_grass` stays in PBR / CS by hand (AP9). The preset Target dropdown lists from the base, cs, vanilla. The set editor's 8-card limit (AO7) is untouched: the complex `_m` keeps its own card under the slot cards.
- **AZ7 Messages.** `TAG-SRGB-VANILLA` reads "_SRGB is not usable under vanilla shaders [D#4]". `PACK-UNUSED-INPUT` reasons say "the complex _m" where they said "the cpm _m".
- **AZ8 Byte identity.** The goldens and packing tests that ran under `Target::Cpm` run under vanilla with `set:<base>.shader=complex_material`. Checked against main 9fbe6bf: its `sdc --target cpm` and this branch's `sdc --target vanilla` with the set shaders on a 28-file fixture (12 raw-map sets, a loose complex `_m`, diffuse, glow, normal) wrote 16 of 16 identical DDS files, plain and with `packing.fill.height`/`packing.fill.metallic`; plain `--target vanilla` wrote 12 of 12 identical files on both.
- **AZ9 Docs.** README, wiki (Presets-and-Targets, CLI-Reference, GUI-Guide, Troubleshooting, FAQ, Home, Getting-Started, Project-Config, Texture-Types, Mod-Scanning), gui.md and presets-changelog describe two targets. Plans, specs and RULINGS are historical and keep `cpm`.

## Note BA — Head parts are head in the 3D preview (owner, 2026-10-15)

- **BA1 Rule.** Community Shaders treats every geometry under a `BSFaceGenNiNode` as head (skin `bodyTiling` = 1). That node exists only in game, so the preview counts a shape as head when ANY of: (1) its shader type is FaceGen (4), as before; (2) its NIF path is under `meshes/actors/character/facegendata/`; (3) its NIF is the model (MODL) of an HDPT record in the scanned plugins. Rules 2 and 3 are per NIF: every shape of such a NIF is head (type-5 mouths, teeth, etc.; RenderDoc capture 11178 ev 4269). The type-5 "counts as body" shortcut is gone.
- **BA2 Any HDPT.** Rule 3 matches any HDPT copy in the active plugins, not only winners: a mesh an overridden head part used to name is still a head mesh.
- **BA3 No schema bump.** The esp reader has captured HDPT MODL into `model_ref` since the first payload commit (78fb141, before schema 8), so existing stores already hold the rows; `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 8 and no rescan is forced. No scan or no plugins = rule 3 false.
- **BA4 Plumbing.** `modscan::Store::is_head_part_mesh(norm)` answers rules 2–3 once per mesh load (Meshes view `mesh_spec`, set preview `load_nif` off the UI thread); the GUI passes it as `MeshSpec::head_part` / `ParsedNif::head_part`; `render3d` keeps it per loaded NIF (`is_head` = rule 1 or the flag). No per-frame query.

## Note BB — Memory admission counts RNM layers and complex grass (owner, 2026-10-15)

Owner go: a layered normal was admitted at its base size and complex grass at one image, so a batch could run several heavy jobs at once. A job's admitted bytes are now the base plus its peak extra working set. The three `shortcut:` comments (pipeline RNM blend, `BakeJob::peak_bytes`, `grass::build`) are gone.

- **BB1 RNM layer term.** `RnmJob::admission_bytes(spec, base)`: the largest of the blend's files (`RnmJob::inputs`: layers, used masks, used AO) by `bake::decode_peak` (the bake's existing model, lifted out of `BakeJob::estimated_bytes`: header dims × (decoded width + 16 + 16), PSD 0 + 32), plus one base-sized float image (16 B/texel) for its resample. Header only, never a decode. 0 when no header reads (the load fails on its own). An 8K layer on a 2K `_n` now admits the 8K decode.
- **BB2 Where it applies.** `api::job_bytes` adds it for `job.active_rnm()` at `resize::target_dims(input, resize)` (the blend runs on the preprocessed, resized base). `BakeJob::estimated_bytes` adds it for the bake's `rnm` at the `_n`'s resized size, since `load_lengths` blends the layers too. Both are sums, not maxes: the base or `_n` decode and the layer decode do not overlap in time, so this over-admits on purpose ("base + peak extra"). The mask and AO planes (4 B/base texel each, held while a layer loads) are not counted separately; the generic 3× base model already over-counts at blend time (only the 16 B/texel work image is alive then).
- **BB3 AV4 unaffected.** Plan pass 1's cache hit admits at 0 and stays correct: planning only stamps layer files (`rnm::plan`), it never decodes one, so a cached base hides no layer cost there. The layer term is a run-time term, and `run` has no `AnalysisCache`, so it reads one header per layer file per job; the cache's dims are not reused (one header read is cheaper than plumbing the cache into `run`).
- **BB4 Complex grass.** `job_bytes` adds one float chain (texels × 16 × 1.34) for `Subtype::ComplexGrass`: in `grass::build` both half Works, both half chains (one full chain together) and the stacked chain are live at once, one chain more than the generic model. That is about 1.33× the generic admission (tested inside 1.3–1.5×).
- **BB5 Scheduling.** `Budget::acquire` is unchanged: the bigger jobs simply run with fewer neighbours, and a job over the whole budget is admitted alone when nothing else is in flight, as any oversized file was, so it cannot deadlock (tested).
- **BB6 Bytes.** Admission only; goldens and output bytes unchanged.

## Note BC — Audit REPORT 2026-10-16 fixes, engine and CLI (owner, 2026-10-16)

Fixes from the owner-commissioned audit (`F:/ai/dds/REPORT.md`, made at main b5ad038; each re-checked on 395012a). One commit per finding.

- **BC1 Closed stdout pipe (#1).** Every stdout print in `crates/cli/src/verbs.rs` (and `say`) goes through `out!`/`out_raw!`, which ignore write errors, so `sdc validate --json | head -n1` exits with the report's code, not 3. Tested with stdout closed before sdc writes.
- **BC2 In-place output root (#3).** `discover` skips the output root only when it is strictly inside the input folder (case-insensitive both ways). An output root equal to the input folder (`-o` = the input, writing beside the sources) or above it skips nothing, where it used to find zero inputs and exit 0. The S6 guard is unchanged and now reachable in place: a re-encoded DDS that would overwrite itself fails and its bytes stay (tested). A later in-place run sees its own `.dds` outputs as DDS inputs, which are skipped (`IN-DDS-SKIPPED`) unless re-encode is opted in.
- **BC3 Complex grass coverage (#4).** A complex-grass job has no base coverage: its top half is preserved in `grass::build` and its bottom half's alpha is specular data, so coverage measured on the stacked image raised a false `VAL-COVERAGE` (tested with specular alternating 100/160). The top half's coverage is not validated separately.
